<heading><span class="col_1241-1242" refersTo="page_0659"/>FRIDAY, 26 MAY 1989</heading>

<heading>HOUSE OF ASSEMBLY</heading>

<heading>QUESTIONS</heading>

<p>&#x2020;Indicates translated version.</p>

<p><i>For oral reply:</i></p>

<p><i>Written replies to questions set down for oral reply on Tuesday, 30 May 1989:</i></p>

<p><i>General Affairs:</i></p>

<oralStatements>

<heading>National roads: privatisation</heading>

<question by="#hardingham" to="#minister_of_transport_affairs">

<num>*2.</num>

<from>Mr R W HARDINGHAM</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Transport Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether it is the intention of his Department to privatise all national roads; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether such privatisation will cover the full distance of each national road;</li>

<li>(3) whether he will make a statement on the matter?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1040E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_transport_affairs" to="#hardingham">

<from>The MINISTER OF TRANSPORT AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.</li>

<li>(2) and (3) Fall away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<oralStatements>

<heading>Teachers referred to in replies: categories</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>*4.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<p>Whether the two teachers referred to in his reply to Question No 295 on 11 May 1989 fall into the same category as do the eight teachers listed in his reply to Question No 22 on 14 March 1989; if not, (a) in what category do these two teachers fall and (b) what are the differences between the categories concerned; if so, why were their names not added to the list of eight teachers?</p>

<p>B1105E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<p>No.</p>

<ol>

<li>(a) Teachers without a professional qualification.</li>

<li>(b) Qualified teachers are those who comply with the minimum professional qualification requirement. Non-qualified teachers are those who are professionally unqualified.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</oralStatements>

<p><i>For written reply:</i></p>

<p><i>General Affairs:</i></p>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Number of houses built for Blacks in specifiedareas</heading>

<question by="#walsh" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>228.</num>

<from>Mr J J WALSH</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<p>What was the total number of houses built for Blacks in the 1987-88 and 1988-89 financial years, respectively, in (a) Cape Town, (b) Durban, (c) Pietermaritzburg, (d) Pretoria, (e) Port Elizabeth, (f) Kimberley, (g) East London, (h) Bloemfontein and (i) Johannesburg?</p>

<p>B513E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#walsh">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>The State does not build homes in Black local authority areas. Funds are however made available to Black local authorities via the National Housing Fund as loans for mainly the development of infrastructure and for material loans to promote selfbuild schemes.</p>

<p>The following amounts have been allocated in respect of the cities mentioned, namely &#x2014;</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p><i>1987/88</i></p></td>

<td><p><i>1988/89</i></p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>&#x2014; Infrastructure</p></td>

<td><p>69 122 450</p></td>

<td><p>44 380 598</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>&#x2014; Material loans</p></td>

<td><p>6 519 019</p></td>

<td><p>7 484 195</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>Reliable statistics regarding the construction of houses by the private sector are not readily available.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Public service: differential pay scales</heading>

<question by="#burrows" to="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation">

<num>237.</num>

<from>Mr R M BURROWS</from>

<p>asked the Minister for Administration and Privatisation:</p>

<p>Whether there are differential pay scales in the Public Service for different grades of employees; if so, (a) how many pay scales are there and (b) what appropriate key scale is applicable to each pay level?</p>

<p>B542E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation" to="#burrows">

<from><span class="col_1243-1244" refersTo="page_0660"/>The MINISTER FOR ADMINISTRATION AND PRIVATISATION:</from>

<p>Yes.</p>

<ol>

<li>(a) 318</li>

</ol>

</answer>

<heading>Housing scheme for Blacks</heading>

<question by="#walsh" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>262.</num>

<from>Mr J J WALSH</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many housing schemes for Blacks are at present being developed in the Western Cape by the (a) State and (b) private sector;</li>

<li>(2) (a) when (i) was each of these schemes initiated and (ii) is it anticipated that each of them will be completed and (b) how many housing units are involved in each case;</li>

<li>(3) whether any housing schemes for lower-income groups are under construction in the Western Cape; if not, why not; if so, (a) (i) how many, (ii) (aa) how many units, and (bb) which companies, are involved in each case and (b) in respect of what date is this information furnished?</li>

</ol>

<p>B567E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#walsh">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>The hon member is referred to my reply to Question 228.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Dulela School: SAP members present at meeting</heading>

<question by="#cronj&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_law_and_order">

<num>264.</num>

<from>Mr P C CRONJ&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Law and Order:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether members of the South African Police were present at a meeting held at the Dulela School in Haza near Howick on or about 19 March 1989; if so, (a) why, (b) which organization called the meeting, (c) when did the meeting (i) commence and (ii) end, (d) at what time did the Police (i) arrive and (ii) depart from the scene of the meeting and (e) how many persons attended the meeting;</li>

<li>(2) whether any persons attending the meeting were armed; if so, (a) what arms did they carry and (b) (i) what steps did the Police take in this regard and (ii) when were they taken;</li>

<li>(3) whether, after the meeting, groups of persons attacked one another; if so, (a) what groups, (b) how many persons were</li>

<li>(i) killed and (ii) seriously injured and (c) what damage was done to property;</li>

<li>(4) whether the Police took any steps to restore order; if not, why not; if so, what steps;</li>

<li>(5) whether any persons were arrested as a result of these incidents; if so, what are the relevant details?</li>

</ol>

<p>B576E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_law_and_order" to="#cronj&#x00E9;">

<from>The MINISTER OF LAW AND ORDER:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) to (e) The &#x201C;Induna&#x201D; of the area concerned which had been appointed by the KwaZulu Government, convened the meeting. The Police arrived at the meeting place at approximately 10h00 to monitor the proceedings. The meeting started at approximately 111130 and was attended by a varying number of persons. The persons finally dispersed at approximately 15h10 and the Police withdrew from the meeting place at approximately 15h55.</li>

</ol></li>

<li><span class="col_1245-1246" refersTo="page_0661"/>(2) (a) and (b) The persons were armed with traditional weapons such as kieries, shields and sticks seeing that the meeting was supposed to be a traditional meeting. No steps were taken against these persons. After the meeting adjourned, persons who moved to the Haza area were dispersed.</li>

<li>(3) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) Presumably supporters of Inkatha and the UDF.</li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) One person.</li>

<li>(ii) Three persons.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(c) None as far as could be ascertained.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(4) Yes. The persons were dispersed.</li>

<li>(5) While the persons were being dispersed, the honourable member of Greytown was observed between them. His presence between the two groups of persons impeded the task of the Police. He was requested to furnish his name and address to the Police, but refused to comply with the request, whereafter he was arrested. He identified himself after his arrest. He was then requested to leave the area in the interest of his own safety. Because he failed to comply with this request, he was taken from the area under police escort and was released.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<heading>Public Service bursaries: applications/awards</heading>

<question by="#burrows" to="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation">

<num>300.</num>

<from>Mr R M BURROWS</from>

<p>asked the Minister for Administration and Privatisation:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) How many applications were made in 1988 for Public Service bursaries for 1989 and (b) what was the race classification of each applicant;</li>

<li>(2) (a) how many awards of Public Service bursaries were made in 1988 and 1989 and (b) what was the race classification of each recipient;</li>

<li>(3) how many such (a) applications were received from and (b) awards were made to Blacks in South Africa, excluding self-governing territories and independent Black states;</li>

<li>(4) what criteria are applied in awarding Public Service bursaries?</li>

</ol>

<p>B640E</p>

<li>(4) The criteria are:

<ol>

<li>(i) Applicability of main field of study/ major subjects/ancillary subjects.</li>

<li>(ii) Educational achievements, namely&#x2014;

<ul>

<li>&#x2022; The number of subjects passed in standard 9 or standard 10, and where applicable the number of subjects passed at university; and</li>

<li>&#x2022; the manner in which each subject was passed.</li>

</ul></li>

<li>(iii) The financial ability of the candidate or his parents.</li>

<li>(iv) Aptitude for occupations in certain fields.</li>

<li>(v) Parental involvement as employee in the Public Service.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

<heading>Bophuthatswana: subsidies received byentrepreneurs/companies</heading>

<question by="#eglin" to="#minister_of_foreign_affairs">

<num>307.</num>

<from>Mr C W EGLIN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Foreign Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any entrepreneurs/companies received subsidies from the South African Government to encourage them to establish industries in Bophuthatswana in 1988; if so,</li>

<li>(2) in respect of each such entrepreneur/ company, (a) what is its name, (b) where is it located, (c) what is the nature of its operation, (d) what amount in aid did it receive in the 1987-88 financial year and (e) for how many years is the South African Government committed to subsidize it?</li>

</ol>

<p>B647E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_foreign_affairs" to="#eglin">

<from>The MINISTER OF FOREIGN AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) (a), (b), (c) and (d)</li>

</ol>

<p>Subsidies amounting to R12 863 905 in total were paid during the 1987/88 financial year to entrepreneurs/companies to encourage them to establish industries in Bophuthatswana. Although I have given more detailed information on each entrepreneur/company in previous years, I should point out that a special computer program has to be used and thousands of transactions have to be keyed in. The Decentralisation Board, which is primarily responsible for the matter, has also indicated that the Board is not in favour of the publication of the finer details. Should it, however, be of special importance to the Honourable Member to obtain the information, I shall make arrangements to provide it to the Honourable Member.</p>

<p>(e)</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Subsidy type</p></td>

<td><p>Period committed</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Interest and rental</p></td>

<td><p>10 years</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Wages</p></td>

<td><p>7 years</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Housing</p></td>

<td><p>20 years</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Railage</p></td>

<td><p>No limit</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Training</p></td>

<td><p>No limit</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Electricity</p></td>

<td><p>No limit</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Squatters in specified areas</heading>

<question by="#walsh" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>331.</num>

<from>Mr J J WALSH</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether his Department or the provincial administrations have any estimates of the number of squatters in the (a) PWV area, (b) Western Cape, (c) Eastern Cape and (d) Durban/Pinetown area; if so, what was the estimated number of squatters in each of these areas as at 31 December 1988;</li>

<li>(2) whether any of these squatters are on official waiting lists for housing; if so, (a) how many in each area and (b) when is it anticipated that sufficient housing will have been provided for these squatters in each area?</li>

</ol>

<p>B674E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#walsh">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>Normally, in certain circles, no destinction is made between individuals on land not intended for residential purposes and persons residing in backyards. Both groups are referred to as squatters. In order not to distort the position an official distinction is made between illegal squatters and backyard residents.</p>

<p>Backyard residents should be dealt with in terms of legislation controlling overcrowding because those concerned are often members of extended families or other lodgers (tenants). Illegal squatters, however, should be dealt with in terms of legislation on squatting.</p>

<p>It must be taken into consideration that the squatting situation is dynamic and can sometimes change dramatically.</p>

<p>Nevertheless, the following particulars have been furnished by the different Provincial Governments:</p>

<table>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>SAP: action against citizens who advocate sanctions</heading>

<question by="#derby-lewis" to="#minister_of_law_and_order">

<num>338.</num>

<from>Mr C J DERBY-LEWIS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Law and Order:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the South African Police have taken and/or intend taking action against South African citizens who advocate sanctions against South Africa; if not, why not; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether the Police intend taking action against a certain person, whose name has been furnished to the South African Police for the purpose of the Minister&#x2019;s reply; if not, why not; if so, (a) what action and (b) when;</li>

<li>(3) what is the name of this person?</li>

</ol>

<p>B735E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_law_and_order" to="#derby-lewis">

<from>The MINISTER OF LAW AND ORDER:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.</li>

<li>(2) A charge in terms of section 54(2)(j) of the Internal Security Act, 1982 (Act 74 of 1982) was investigated and the case docket submitted to the Attorney-General for a decision. He declined to institute prosecution.

<ol>

<li>(a) and (b) Fall away.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3) The name which was furnished by the honourable member.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Bank officials involved in foreign exchangefrauds</heading>

<question by="#schoeman" to="#minister_of_law_and_order">

<num>342.</num>

<from>Mr C B SCHOEMAN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Law and Order:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any investigations by Commercial Branch detectives of the <span class="col_1259-1260" refersTo="page_0668"/>South African Police into alleged foreign exchange frauds involving officials of banks have exposed criminal negligence on the part of the banks concerned; if so,</li>

<li>(2) whether any criminal charges have been laid against such banks; if not, why not; if so, what are the relevant details?</li>

</ol>

<p>B697E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_law_and_order" to="#schoeman">

<from>The MINISTER OF LAW AND ORDER:</from>

<p>(1) and (2)</p>

<p>Various charges of fraud and contraventions of the Foreign Exchange Regulations are being investigated. With the exception of one case, the investigations have not yet been completed. In view of the fact that the investigations could be jeopardized and the judicial process anticipated, no information regarding these cases is furnished at this stage.</p>

<p>The case against the African Bank and three bank officials has been finalized. The bank, in its capacity as corporate body, was acquitted. The three bank officials were convicted and senteced on 99 charges of fraud and 6 charges in terms of the Foreign Exchange Regulations.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Journalists from Eastern bloc countries: visit toRSA</heading>

<question by="#van_wyk" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>351.</num>

<from>Mr W J D VAN WYK</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:</p>

<p>Whether any applications were received over the latest specified period of 12 months for which figures are available from journalists from Eastern bloc countries wishing to visit or work in South Africa; if so, (a) (i) how many applications were received and (ii) from which countries were these applicants and (b) (i) how many such applications were granted and (ii) (aa) to whom and (bb) for what periods were they granted in each case?</p>

<p>B723E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#van_wyk">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<p>Yes.</p>

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) The following applications were received by the Department of Home Affairs during the 12 months ending 31 March 1989:<br/>

Vacation &#x2014; 5<br/>

Transit &#x2014; 5<br/>

Employment &#x2014;12</li>

<li>(ii) East Germany;<br/>

Union of Socialist Soviet Republics;<br/>

People&#x2019;s Republic of China;<br/>

Yugoslavia;<br/>

Poland; and<br/>

Hungary</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) 15 applications were approved during the aforesaid period. Particulars are as follows:<br/>

Vacation &#x2014; 5<br/>

Transit &#x2014; 4<br/>

Employment* &#x2014; 6<br/>

5 applications &#x2014; 1 for transit and 4 for employment &#x2014; were still under consideration at 31 March 1989.<br/>

* Reporting on specific subjects in limited time.</li>

<li>(ii) (aa) and (bb) It is not considered expedient to disclose information of this nature as a visa application is a personal matter between the applicant and the Department of Home Affairs.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading><span class="col_1263-1264" refersTo="page_0670"/>Public Service employees: salary differences</heading>

<question by="#burrows" to="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation">

<num>357.</num>

<from>Mr R M BURROWS</from>

<p>asked the Minister for Administration and Privatisation:</p>

<p>Whether there are differences in the salaries paid and/or fringe benefits awarded to employees in the Public Service in comparison with those received by persons in the Public Service who were previously in the employ of the Development Boards; if so, (a) what are these differences, (b) why do such differences exist and (c) for how long will they continue to exist?</p>

<p>B759E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation" to="#burrows">

<from>The MINISTER FOR ADMINISTRATION AND PRIVATISATION:</from>

<p>Yes.</p>

<p>(a) <i>Salary</i></p>

<p>The salaries/scales payable at the former Development Boards prior to take-over, were adjusted to the next higher notch on the salary key scale of the Public Service on the transfer of the personnel to the Public Service and were retained as such for an <i>interim</i> period. The allocated salaries/scales were in certain cases lower, and in other cases higher, than those of the ranks in the Public Service in which they were appointed.</p>

<p><i>Pension fund</i></p>

<p>Personnel of the former Development Boards could, on their transfer to the Public Service, elect to remain members of the pension funds of which they were members prior to the transfer. Personnel in the Public Service can only be members of either the Government Service Pension Fund or the Pension Fund for Temporary Employees.</p>

<p><i>Retirement age</i></p>

<p>Personnel of the former Development Boards retained their retirement age on transfer to the Public Service, which could be as early as 55 years in some cases. Personnel in the Public Service can retire from age 55 (females)/60 (males) if they were appointed in the Public Service prior to 24 June 1955. Those appointed in the Public Service on or after that date can only retire at the age of 65 years.</p>

<p><i>Group life insurance scheme</i></p>

<p>A subsidised group life insurance scheme was maintained for personnel of the former Development Boards after their transfer to the Public Service. Personnel in the Public Service do not enjoy such a benefit.</p>

<p><i>Severance pay benefit</i></p>

<p>Personnel of the former Development Boards who were temporary and who were transferred to the Public Service, retained the benefit of the payment of a cash amount in the termination of their services which they enjoyed at some of the boards. Temporary personnel in the Public Service do not enjoy such a benefit.</p>

<p><i>Vacation leave payment</i></p>

<p>Personnel of the former Development Boards can, when they resign from the Public Service, be paid out the cash value of the number of days vacation leave which stood to their credit prior to the date of transfer. Payment is calculated on the salary which they received on the day prior to the date of transfer. Personnel in the Public Service do not receive payment for their accumulated vacation leave when they resign.</p>

<p><i>Housing allowance</i></p>

<p>Personnel of the former Development Boards retained, on transfer, the housing allowances which were paid to them at that time for the hire of private houses for a maximum of 2 years. Personnel in the Public Service do not enjoy such a benefit.</p>

<p><i>Long service recognition</i></p>

<p>In addition to long service awards which apply to personnel in the Public Service, personnel of the former Development Boards retained the next long service award which they would have received from the boards. This non-recurring award is, depending on the measures which the particular board applied, a percentage of salary or can take the form of additional vacation leave.</p>

<p><i>Subsidised car loan schemes</i></p>

<p>Personnel of the former Development Boards continued to participate, after transfer, in existing subsidised car loan schemes for the remaining duration of their loan period. These schemes are in some cases more, and in other cases less <span class="col_1265-1266" refersTo="page_0671"/>favourable than the scheme applicable to personnel in the Public Service.</p>

<p><i>Allowances</i></p>

<p>Specific individuals and a few personnel groups of the former Development Boards retained on transfer allowances which they received for particular circumstances, for which no equivalent existed in the Public Service (for example uniform allowances and drivers&#x2019; allowances).</p>

<p>(b) <i>Salary</i></p>

<p>The adjusted salaries/scales which were allocated on transfer, were only retained for an <i>interim</i> period, until the correct rank and salary/scale could be awarded in terms of the criteria applicable to the determination of salaries and coupling of ranks, in those cases where the adjusted salaries/scales were lower than the correct ranks and salaries/scales. In terms of section 8(a) of the Abolition of Development Bodies Act, 1986 (Act 75 of 1986), the salary/scale of a person in the service of a Development Board could not be decreased on his transfer to and appointment in the Public Service. Personnel with higher salaries/scales than the Public Service salaries/scales therefore had to retain the higher salaries/scales.</p>

<p><i>Other differences</i></p>

<p>The Act also determines that a person shall not, as a consequence of his transfer to and appointment in the Public Service, be granted a <i>retirement age</i> which is less favourable than that which applied to him in the service of the development body and that a person shall after his transfer be granted a non-recurring choice to either <i>remain a member of the pension fund</i> of which he was a member, or to <i>become a member of the pension fund</i> applicable to officers or employees in the Public Service.</p>

<p>The temporary or permanent retention of the undermentioned benefits was necessary to ensure reasonable and fair treatment of those concerned and because an outright abolition of the benefits could have brought about inconvenience or financial hardship:</p>

<ul><li>* Group life insurance scheme</li>

<li>* Severance pay benefit</li>

<li>* Vacation leave payment</li>

<li>* Housing allowances</li>

<li>* Long service recognition</li>

<li>* Subsidised car loan scheme</li>

<li>* Allowances.</li>

</ul>

<p>(c) <i>Salary</i></p>

<p>Cases where a lower adjusted salary/scale than the correct Public Service salary/ scale were awarded, were finally phased out with effect from 1 November 1988. Cases where the adjusted salaries/scales of former Development Board personnel are higher than those of their Public Service counterparts, will be phased out during future occupational specific salary adjustments.</p>

<p><i>Other differences</i></p>

<p>The following differences will phase out only at termination of services:</p>

<ul><li>* Pension fund membership</li>

<li>* Retirement age</li>

<li>* Group life insurance scheme</li>

<li>* Severance pay benefit</li>

<li>* Vacation leave payment.</li>

</ul>

<p>The following differences will phase out after a prescribed period or after the circumstances for the receipt of the benefit have changed:</p>

<ul><li>* Housing allowance</li>

<li>* Long service recognition</li>

<li>* Subsidised car loan scheme</li>

<li>* Allowances.</li>

</ul>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Senior certificate examinations</heading>

<heading>Increase in incidence of notifiable diseases</heading>

<question by="#ellis" to="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development">

<num>371.</num>

<from>Mr M J ELLIS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of National Health and Population Development:</p>

<ol>

<li><span class="col_1275-1276" refersTo="page_0676"/>(1) Whether there was any increase in the incidence of (a) malaria, (b) typhoid and (c) cholera in the Republic in 1988; if so, (i) to what extent, (ii) in which areas and (iii) how many cases of each disease were diagnosed in each area;</li>

<li>(2) whether any persons died of these diseases during this period; if so, how many in each race group in each area in respect of each disease;</li>

<li>(3) whether any steps are being taken to combat the spread of these diseases; if so, what steps in each specified area?</li>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development" to="#ellis">

<ol>

<li>(1)

<li>(a) No,

<li>(i), (ii) and (iii) fall away,</li>

<li>(b) no,

<ol>

<li>(i), (ii) and (iii) fall away,</li>

<ol>

<li>(i), (ii) and (iii) fall away;</li>

</ol></li>

<p>no deaths from cholera were notified in 1988;</p>

<ol>

<li>(3) yes, as soon as a local authority is notified about an infectious- or communicable disease, measures are taken to prevent the spread which include

<ul><li>&#x2014; identification and tracing of the source of infection;</li>

<li>&#x2014; elimination/&#x00ED;solation/removal of safeguarding of the source;</li>

<li>&#x2014; treatment/prophylactic treatment; and</li>

<li>&#x2014; immunisation if applicable.</li>

</ul></li>

</ol>

<p>Regulations for the prevention, restriction of and control of infectious and communicable diseases exist (R.2438 of 30 October 1987).</p>

<p>In all the malaria endemic areas, the malaria vector mosquitoes are controlled by spraying dwellings and structures with insecticides. In some of the breeding sites larvicides are also applied.</p>

<p>Active surveillance is done to trace and treat people with malaria, especially infiltrators crossing the northern and eastern borders of the RSA.</p>

<p>The inhabitants of and visitors to malaria endemic areas are informed regarding the risk areas, use of prophylaxis, mosquito nets, insect repellents and aerosols to prevent the contraction of malaria.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading><span class="col_1277-1278" refersTo="page_0677"/>SAP: labour disputes/work stoppages</heading>

<question by="#gastrow" to="#minister_of_law_and_order">

<num>373.</num>

<from>Mr P H P GASTROW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Law and Order:</p>

<p>In how many instances were the South African Police called to the scene of (a) labour disputes, (b) work stoppages and (c) strikes in 1988?</p>

<p>B775E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_law_and_order" to="#gastrow">

<from>The MINISTER OF LAW AND ORDER:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) 18 instances</li>

<li>(b) 12 instances</li>

<li>(c) 277 instances.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Industries: decentralization incentive benefits</heading>

<question by="#schwarz" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>374.</num>

<from>Mr H H SCHWARZ</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<p>Whether any industries with decentralization incentive benefits (a) had these incentive benefits (i) removed and (ii) reduced, (b) were place-bound industries, and (c) ceased to operate, in 1988; if so, how many in each case?</p>

<p>B776E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#schwarz">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>Mr H H Schwarz&#x2019;s reference to 1988 is interpreted to refer to the financial year 1 April 1988 to 31 March 1989.</p>

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) Industries of which concessions were withdrawn in the period 1 April 1988 to 31 March 1989, are as follows:<br/>Three undertakings due to technicalities such as non-compliance with the requirements subject to which concessions were granted. (All concessions withdrawn.)<br/>Three so-called place-bound and raw material orientated industries&#x2019; benefits were withdrawn as a final phase of an exercise largely completed in 1987. (All concessions withdrawn.)</li>

<li>(ii) Five undertakings involved in mining related activities had their transport concessions withdrawn, following an investigation of such undertakings.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) No concessions are presently being granted or paid to so-called place-bound and raw material orientated industries.</li>

<li>(c) It is not possible to indicate how many undertakings enjoying concessions, ceased operating in 1988 due to the delay in the submission by industrialists of their claims. According to the Development Corporations, the Decentralization Board&#x2019;s Regional Representativs and Inspectorate, who are constantly in contact with concessionaries, the number of concessionary firms closing down, is however insignificant.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Decentralization Board: applications</heading>

<question by="#schwarz" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>375.</num>

<from>Mr H H SCHWARZ</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many applications were made to the Decentralization Board in 1988;</li>

<li>(2) (a) how many such applications (i) were approved and (ii) failed to meet the requirements and (b) what was the value of the applications in each category;</li>

<li>(3) (a) how many employment opportunities were created in consequence of the approved applications that had been finalized and (b) what estimated number of employment opportunities will be created in consequence of such applications;</li>

<li>(4) what was the total (a) number and (b) value of the applications involving foreign investors?</li>

</ol>

<p>B777E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#schwarz">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) During the period 1 April 1988 to 31 March 1989, 1 106 applications for regional industrial incentives were received by the Decentralization Board in respect of the RSA and the Self-governing Territories.</li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) Of these applications (i) 1 042 were successful while (ii) 64 did not comply with the Board&#x2019;s requirements and were consequently turned down.</li>

<li>(b) The approved applications represent an anticipated capital investment of R2,2 milliard. No data is readily available in respect of the applications which were turned down.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3)

<ol>

<li>(a) Due to the fact that it can take up to two years for an approved project to establish, it is not possible at this stage to indicate how many of the <span class="col_1279-1280" refersTo="page_0678"/>recently approved projects will eventually establish and how many job opportunities will be created.</li>

<li>(b) The estimated number of employment opportunities expected to be created as a result of the applications approved in 1988/89, amounts to 70 721.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(4)

<ol>

<li>(a) The total number of applications involving foreign investors, amounts to 115 for the year, which represents an increase of 24 per cent over the previous year.</li>

<li>(b) The total investment involved in these foreign projects, amounts to R700 million, whilst the expected number of employment opportunities is put as 22 130.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Decentralization/deconcentration : amount paid</heading>

<question by="#schwarz" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>376.</num>

<from>Mr H H SCHWARZ</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<p>What was the total amount paid out in respect of decentralization or deconcentration benefits in 1988?</p>

<p>B778E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#schwarz">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>The total amount spent on the Regional Industrial Development Incentive Scheme during the period 1 April 1988 to 31 March 1989, amounted to R610,7 million.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Identity documents: applications</heading>

<question by="#van_der_merwe" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>386.</num>

<from>Mr S S VAN DER MERWE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What number of applications for identity documents was outstanding in respect of (a) Whites, (b) Coloureds, (c) Indians and (d) Blacks as at 31 December 1988;</li>

<li>(2) whether there is a delay in the issuing of identity documents at present; if so, (a) why, (b) how long should it take to process each document and (c) what is the (i) average and (ii) maximum delay between the receipt of an application and the issuing of a document in respect of each race group?</li>

</ol>

<p>B788E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#van_der_merwe">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) 11 134</li>

<li>(b) 12 108</li>

<li>(c) 4 592</li>

<li>(d) 276 577</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) In the normal course of events, it takes at present approximately ten weeks to process an application for an identity document and to mail the identity document to the applicant. Unfortunately, delays are experienced in the processing of incomplete applications. Such delays are to a large extent influenced by the time the applicant takes to respond to the Department&#x2019;s enquiries. It is, therefore, not possible to calculate an average and maximum delay between the receipt of an application and the issuing of an identity document.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Black townships: proclaimed/deproclaimed</heading>

<question by="#walsh" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>390.</num>

<from>Mr J J WALSH</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) How many Black townships outside the self-governing territories were (i) proclaimed and (ii) deproclaimed in 1988 and (b) where are these townships situated;</li>

<li>(2) how many persons moved into the proclaimed townships in 1988?</li>

</ol>

<p>B792E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#walsh">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrator of the different provinces and they have furnished the following information:</p>

<p><i>Transvaal:</i></p>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) Number of applications for Black township establishment &#x2014;

<ul><li>&#x2014; received in 1988 148</li>

<li>&#x2014; approved in 1988 103</li>

<li>&#x2014; declared in 1988 5</li>

</ul></li>

<li>(ii) Number of Black townships deproclaimed: None</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) The townships are situated as follows:</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

<num>391.</num>

<from>Mr J J WALSH</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<p>Whether his Department spent any money in the 1988-89 financial year on the construction of houses for Blacks in (a) Cape Town, (b) Durban, (c) Pietermaritzburg, (d) Pretoria, (e) Port Elizabeth, (f) Kimberley, (g) East London, (h) Bloemfontein and (i) Johannesburg; if not, why not; if so, what amount in each case?</p>

<p>B793E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#walsh">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>The hon member is referred to my reply to Question 228.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Prudence Secondary School: art examinationscripts</heading>

<question by="#van_der_merwe" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>392.</num>

<from>Mr S S VAN DER MERWE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether all the art examination scripts of Standard 6 pupils at the Prudence Secondary School in Tladi, Soweto, were marked at the end of 1988; if so, how many (a) passed and (b) failed the examination; if not, (i) why not and (ii) what action has been taken in this regard;</li>

<li>(2) whether any pupils whose scripts were not marked failed Standard 6; if so, what are the relevant details;</li>

<li><span class="col_1285-1286" refersTo="page_0681"/>(3) whether any pupils allocated to Class 6H in Room 42 passed; if not, (a) why not, (b) how many scripts were (i) marked and (ii) not marked and (c) who was responsible for (i) invigilating at this examination and (ii) marking these scripts?</li>

</ol>

<p>B814E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#van_der_merwe">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.

<ol>

<li>(i) The whole of the Std. 6H-class attended no classes in Art after 16 March 1988 despite the principal&#x2019;s exhortations to both the pupils and their parents for the pupils to do so. As a result they had failed before the examination began because they had no year-mark for practical work. Although they did not qualify to write the examination, they coerced the invigilator into letting them write. Their scripts were not marked, however.</li>

<li>(ii) In 1989 the pupils were allowed to repeat the year and to take Art.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Yes. Thirty one of the 35 Std. 6H-class pupils failed because they had failed three or more subjects in addition to Art. The other four passed in spite of having failed Art.</li>

<li>(3) Yes. Four pupils passed Std 6.

<ol>

<li>(a) Falls away</li>

<li>(b) (i) and (ii) Fall away</li>

<li>(c) (i) and (ii) Fall away.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Transport of out-patients from Gardens area</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development">

<num>393.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of National Health and Population Development:</p>

<p>Whether, in 1989, any changes are planned in the (a) provision of, (b) charges for and (c) conditions relating to the transport of outpatients from the Gardens area to (i) Groote Schuur Hospital and (ii) other Cape Peninsula hospitals for (aa) ordinary, (bb) bed-ridden and (cc) wheelchair patients; if so, (i) what changes, (ii) when will they come into effect, (iii) why are they being introduced, (iv) who decided upon them and (v) what is the amount of the savings anticipated?</p>

<p>B816E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL HEALTH AND POPULATION DEVELOPMENT:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) and (c) (i) and (ii) Yes,

<ol>

<li>(aa)

<ol>

<li>(i) only patients who are definitely medically unfit to make use of public transport, and not for indigent or welfare reasons, will be conveyed,</li>

<li>(ii) as soon as the new contract has been finalised,</li>

<li>(iii) to save on costs (conveyance of patients because of their poor financial position only, is not a function of the Administration),</li>

<li>(iv) the Administrator-in-Executive Committee on recommendation of the Executive Director: Hospital and Health Services,</li>

<li>(v) approximately R1 million to R1,5 million per annum for the entire Cape Peninsula. The saving for the Gardens area will be small since relatively few patients were conveyed from and to this area in the past,</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(bb)

<ol>

<li>(i) no,</li>

<li>(ii), (iii), (iv) and (v) fall away,</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(cc)

<ol>

<li>(i) no,</li>

<li>(ii), (iii), (iv) and (v) fall away,</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) (i) and (ii) yes,

<ol>

<li>(aa)

<ol>

<li>(i)

<ul><li>&#x2014; from R1 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient with a monthly income between RO and R275 to R2 for a patient with a monthly income between RO and R315,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R2 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient with a monthly income of more than R275 but who does not yet pay income tax, to R3 for a patient with a monthly income of more than R315 but who does not yet pay income tax,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R3,40 per single journey of 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying between RO and R84 income tax per annum to <span class="col_1287-1288" refersTo="page_0682"/>R4 for a patient paying between RO and R96 income tax per annum,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R5,65 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying between R84 and R167 income tax per annum to R8 for a patient paying between R96 and R192 income tax per annum,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R9,10 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying between R167 and R251 income tax per annum to R13 for a patient paying between R192 and R288 income tax per annum,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R11,35 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying between R251 and R334 income tax per annum to R17 for a patient paying between R288 and R384 income tax per annum,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R13,60 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying more than R334 income tax per annum to R33 for a patient paying more than R384 income tax per annum,</li>

</ul></li>

<li>(ii) 1 May 1989,</li>

<li>(iii) to keep up with the increased medical aid tariffs and thus ensure that the State receives its just income,</li>

<li>(iv) the Treasury on recommendation by the Administrator-in-Executive Committee;</li>

<li>(v) an indeterminable expected higher income,</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(bb) and (cc)

<ol>

<li>(i)

<ul><li>&#x2014; from R1 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient with a monthly income between R0 and R275 to R2 for a patient with a monthly income between R0 and R315,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R2 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient with a monthly income of more than R275 but who does not yet pay income tax, to R3 for a patient with a monthly income of more than R315 but who does not yet pay income tax,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R5,65 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying between R0 and R84 income tax per annum to R6 for a patient paying between R0 and R96 income tax per annum,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R11,35 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying between R84 and R167 income tax per annum to R12 for a patient paying between R96 and R192 income tax per annum,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R17 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying between R167 and R251 income tax per annum to R17 for a patient paying between R192 and R288 income tax per annum,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R22,70 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying between R251 and R334 income tax per annum to R23 for a patient paying between R288 and R384 income tax per annum,</li>

<li>&#x2014; from R28,35 per single journey per 50 km or part thereof for a patient paying more than R334 income tax per annum to R46 for a patient paying more than R384 income tax per annum,</li>

</ul></li>

<li>(ii) 1 May 1989,</li>

<li>(iii) to keep up with the increased medical aid tariffs and thus ensure that the State receives its just income,</li>

<li>(iv) the Treasury on recommendation by the Administrator-in-Executive Committee,</li>

<li>(v) an indeterminable expected higher income.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Passports to Black South Africans</heading>

<question by="#schoeman" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>394.</num>

<from>Mr C B SCHOEMAN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:</p>

<p>(a) How many Black South Africans were granted South African passports in each of the latest specified five years for which information <span class="col_1289-1290" refersTo="page_0683"/>is available and (b) (i) how many such Black South Africans (aa) applied for and (bb) were granted visas, and (ii) for which countries were they granted visas, in respect of each of these years?</p>

<p>B698E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#schoeman">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) My Department only keeps record of the total number of passport applications received. Figures for a specific population group are therefore not available.</li>

<li>(b) Visa applications by South Africans wishing to visit overseas countries are handled by the Embassies of the countries concerned. My Department is not involved in the process.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Border between Bophuthatswana and RSA : investigation into smuggling</heading>

<question by="#derby-lewis" to="#minister_of_law_and_order">

<num>399.</num>

<from>Mr C J DERBY-LEWIS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Law and Order:</p>

<p>Whether any instances of smuggling involving across-the-border traffic between Bophuthatswana and the Republic were investigated by the South African Police during the latest specified period of five years for which information is available; if so, (a) how many complaints in this regard were (i) received and (ii) investigated, (b) what products were involved and (c) what was the outcome of these investigations?</p>

<p>B708E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_law_and_order" to="#derby-lewis">

<from>The MINISTER OF LAW AND ORDER:</from>

<p>Yes.</p>

<ol>

<li>(a) (i) and (ii) 87 complaints were received and investigated.</li>

<li>(b) Drugs, liquor and motor vehicles.</li>

<li>(c) In 68 cases the persons were tried, convicted and sentenced.<br/>In 8 cases the persons were acquitted.<br/>In 6 cases the trials have not yet been finalized.<br/>In 5 instances the cases were withdrawn as a result of insufficient evidence.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Entry visas for South Africa</heading>

<question by="#van_wyk" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>404.</num>

<from>Mr W J D VAN WYK</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:</p>

<p>Whether the citizens of certain countries do not require entry visas for South Africa; if so, of what counties?</p>

<p>B726E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#van_wyk">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<p>Yes.</p>

<p>In general the holder of a passport of any of the following countries is presently exempted from visa requirements, provided his visa exemption has not been withdrawn:</p>

<p>United Kingdom</p>

<p>Republic of Ireland</p>

<p>Botswana</p>

<p>Lesotho</p>

<p>Swaziland</p>

<p>Transkei</p>

<p>Bophuthatswana</p>

<p>Venda</p>

<p>Ciskei</p>

<p>Furthermore passport holders of Switzerland, Liechtenstein and the Federal Republic of Germany who wish to visit South Africa for <i>bona fide</i> holiday or business purposes are for such visits exempted from visa requirements.</p>

<p>Visa agreements are also from time to time reached with countries, the details of which it is not considered expedient to disclose in public. If required, such information will be furnished to the hon member confidentially.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading><span class="col_1291-1292" refersTo="page_0684"/>Black war veterans: pensions</heading>

<question by="#burrows" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>411.</num>

<from>Mr R M BURROWS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) How many Black war veterans are receiving their pensions from each provincial administration, (b) what tariff sum is paid in each case and (c) in respect of what date is this information furnished;</li>

<li>(2) what total sum was paid out in such pensions by each provincial administration in the 1988-89 financial year?</li>

</ol>

<p>B832E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#burrows">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter rests in the Administrators of the different provinces and they have furnished the following information:</p>

<p><i>Orange Free State:</i></p>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) 242</li>

<li>(b) R266 monthly</li>

<li>(c) 31 March 1989</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) R617 939</li>

</ol>

<p><i>Natal:</i></p>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Hospitals transferred to own affairsadministration</heading>

<question by="#burrows" to="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development">

<num>412.</num>

<from>Mr R M BURROWS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of National Health and Population Development:</p>

<p>(a) Which hospitals in each province were transferred to each own affairs administration with effect from 1 April 1989 and (b) what is the exact nature of the relationship between these hospitals and the hospital services of the provincial administration in each province?</p>

<p>B833E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development" to="#burrows">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL HEALTH AND POPULATION DEVELOPMENT:</from>

<p><i>Natal:</i></p>

<ol>

<li>(a) The following hospitals were transferred to the Administration: House of Assembly:<br/>

Grey&#x2019;s Hospital, Pietermaritzburg<br/>

Hillcrest Hospital, Hillcrest<br/>

Greytown Hospital (section reserved for White patients).</li>

<li>(b) The Branch: Hospital Services of the Natal Provincial Administration will function as an agent for the Administration: House of Assembly. Although these institutions will be funded by the House of Assembly, the day to day running would still be the responsibility of the Provincial Administration.</li>

</ol>

<p><i>Orange Free State:</i></p>

<ol>

<li>(a) The following hospitals were transferred to the Administration: House of Assembly:<br/>

Voortrekker Hospital, Kroonstad<br/>

Provincial Hospital, Bethlehem (section reserved for White patients)<br/>

Provincial Hospital, Sasolburg<br/>

Provincial Hospital, Jagersfontein (section reserved for White patients)<br/>

Provincial Hospital, Zastron (section reserved for White patients).</li>

<li>(b) The personnel of these institutions are on the establishment of the Provincial Administration, who manages the institutions for the Administration: House of Assembly on an agency basis.</li>

</ol>

<p><span class="col_1293-1294" refersTo="page_0685"/><i>Transvaal:</i></p>

<ol>

<li>(a) The following hospitals were transferred to the Administration: House of Assembly:<br/>

Andrew McColm Hospital, Pretoria<br/>

Bernice Sameul Hospital, Delmas<br/>

Bloemhof Hospital, Bloemhof<br/>

Brits Hospital, Brits<br/>

Delareyville Hospital, Delareyville<br/>

Duiwelskloof Hospital, Duiwelskloof<br/>

Elsie Ballot Hospital, Amersfoort<br/>

Evander Hospital, Evander<br/>

F H Odendaal Hospital, Nylstroom<br/>

Generaal De la Rey Memorial Hospital, Lichtenburg<br/>

Groblersdal Hospital, Groblersdal<br/>

H A Grov&#x00E9; Hospital, Belfast<br/>

Hendrik van der Bijl Hospital, Vanderbijlpark<br/>

J D Verster Hospital, Koster<br/>

J G Strijdom Hospital, Johannesburg<br/>

Kempton Park Hospital, Kempton Park<br/>

Louis Trichardt Memorial Hospital, Louis Trichardt<br/>

Discovery Memorial Hospital, Florida<br/>

Paardekraal Hospital, Krugersdorp<br/>

Phalaborwa Hospital, Phalaborwa<br/>

Pretoria-West Hospital, Pretoria<br/>

Sannieshof Hospital, Sannieshof<br/>

South Rand Hospital, Rosettenville<br/>

Sybrand van Niekerk Hospital, Carletonville<br/>

Van Velden Memorial Hospital, Tzaneen<br/>

Ventersdorp Hospital, Ventersdorp<br/>

Vereeniging Hospital, Vereeniging<br/>

Far East Rand Hospital, Springs<br/>

Voortrekker Hospital, Potgietersrus<br/>

Warmbaths Hospital, Warmbath<br/>

Waterval Boven Hospital, Waterval Boven<br/>

Willem Cruywagen Hospital, Germiston</li>

<li>(b) The Transvaal Provincial Administration runs the hospitals as an agent of the Administration: House of Assembly.</li>

</ol>

<p><i>Cape Province:</i></p>

<ol>

<li>(a) The following hospitals were transferred to the Administration: House of Assembly:<br/>

Williams Slater Hospital, Cape Town<br/>

Volks Hospital, Cape Town<br/>

Provincial Hospital, Port Elizabeth (including the Allan Nash and Algoa Park Clinics)<br/>

Walvisbay Hospital (section reserved for White patients).</li>

<li>(b) The hospitals are run on an agency basis by the Cape Provincial Administration. The personnel remain on the establishment of the Cape Provincial Administration.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>SADF: statistics on refusal to report for service</heading>

<question by="#dalling" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>416.</num>

<from>Mr D J DALLING</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:</p>

<p>Whether the Central Statistical Service keeps statistics on the number of persons who were charged in magistrates&#x2019; courts with failure or refusal to report for service in the South African Defence Force; if not, why not; if so, (a) how many persons were so charged with failure or refusal to report for (i) military service, (ii) Citizen Force camps and (iii) Commando duty in 1988 and (b) what was the outcome in each case?</p>

<p>B837E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#dalling">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<p>The Central Statistical Service does not keep statistics on the basis the hon member has in mind. The statistics kept on offences cover all the more important economic-sociological offences including transgressions of the Defence Act. These transgressions are, however, not recorded or statistically processed in more detail.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Diarrhoea/dysentery: deaths</heading>

<question by="#ellis" to="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development">

<num>421.</num>

<from>Mr M J ELLIS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of National Health and Population Development:</p>

<p>Whether his Department keeps statistics of the number of (a) White, (b) Coloured, (c) Indian and (d) Black children who died of (i) diarrhoea and (ii) dysentery; if not, why not; if so, what are the relevant statistics for 1988?</p>

<p>B842E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development" to="#ellis">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL HEALTH AND POPULATION DEVELOPMENT:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) (i) and (ii); no</li>

<li>(b) (i) and (ii); no</li>

<li>(c) (i) and (ii); no</li>

<li>(d) (i) and (ii); no.</li>

</ol>

<p>The Department of National Health and Population Development does not keep statistics in respect of child deaths caused by diarrhoea or dysentery. The information can be obtained from the Central Statistical Services.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<question by="#malcomess" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>431.</num>

<from>Mr D J N MALCOMESS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether a certain person, whose name has been furnished to the Minister&#x2019;s Department for the purpose of his reply, is employed by the Ibhayi Town Council; if so, (a) what is the name of this person, (b) in what capacity is he employed, (c) (i) when and (ii) for what period was he appointed and (d) what are his qualifications for this post;</li>

<li>(2) whether this post was advertised; if not, (a) why not and (b) why was this person appointed to the post?</li>

</ol>

<p>B852E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#malcomess">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrator of the Cape Province and he has furnished the following information:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.</li>

<li>(2) Falls away.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>New Brighton: contract for building of houses</heading>

<question by="#malcomess" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<from>Mr D J N MALCOMESS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any contract was awarded for the building of houses in New Brighton in the last two years; if so, (a) by whom, (b) to whom and (c) at what price per unit;</li>

<li>(2) whether tenders were invited; if not, (a) why not and (b) what criteria were applied in selecting the company chosen; if so, (i) who submitted tenders and (ii) at what price per unit in each case;</li>

<li>(3) whether the lowest tender was accepted; if not, (a) why not and (b) what criteria were applied in selecting the company chosen?</li>

</ol>

<p>B853E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#malcomess">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrator of the Cape Province and he has furnished the following information:</p>

<p>(1) No. Rest of question falls away.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Special constables: compensation for damages</heading>

<question by="#suzman" to="#minister_of_law_and_order">

<num>440.</num>

<from>Mrs H SUZMAN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Law and Order:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any claims for damages were made against the South African Police for actions taken by special constables in 1988; if so, (a) how many and (b) for what total amount;</li>

<li>(2) whether any insurance cover has been taken out by the Police against claims in respect of alleged unlawful conduct by special constables; if so, what was the total value of the insurance cover taken out in the 1988-89 financial year?</li>

</ol>

<p>B861E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_law_and_order" to="#suzman">

<from>The MINISTER OF LAW AND ORDER:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) to (b)<br/>A number of claims for damages have been received. Several of the cases became prescribed while other cases were withdrawn by the complainants. In one <span class="col_1305-1306" refersTo="page_0691"/>case the claim was dismissed by the court with costs. However, a number of the cases are still continuing and will be finalized in due course. In six of these claims the cases have been settled for a total amount of R42 849,64.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) No.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Madeira: visit by members of Cabinet</heading>

<question by="#lorimer" to="#state_president">

<num>449.</num>

<from>Mr R J LORIMER</from>

<p>asked the State President:</p>

<p>Whether any members and/or former members of the Cabinet visited Madeira in the past three years; if so, (a) what are the names of the members concerned and (b) (i) when, (ii) for what purpose and (iii) in what capacity did each visit Madeira?</p>

<p>B879E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#state_president" to="#lorimer">

<from>The STATE PRESIDENT:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(1) Mr P W Botha</li>

<li>(2) Mr RF Botha</li>

<li>(3) Mr D W Steyn</li>

<li>(4) Mr P T C du Plessis</li>

</ol></li>

<li><span class="col_1309-1310" refersTo="page_0693"/>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i)

<ol>

<li>(1) 13-14 November 1987</li>

<li>(2) 13-14 November 1987</li>

<li>(3) 1-4 May 1988</li>

<li>(4) End of 1986</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(ii)

<ol>

<li>(1) On invitation of the President of Madeira after the opening of the Delvillewood Memorial.</li>

<li>(2) Accompany the State President during the visit as discussed in point (b)(ii)(1).</li>

<li>(3) To discuss matters of bilateral interest and the position of the Porto Santo airport with the President of Madeira.</li>

<li>(4) Private visit during holiday.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(iii)

<ol>

<li>(1) State President</li>

<li>(2) Minister of Foreign Affairs</li>

<li>(3) Minister of Economic Affairs and Technology</li>

<li>(4) Private</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Cape Peninsula: disruptive incidents at secondaryschools</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>453.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether there were any disruptive incidents or clashes between staff and pupils at any secondary schools in the Cape Peninsula during the week ended 21 April 1989; if so, (a) what (i) was the nature and (ii) were the causes of these incidents or clashes, (b) (i) when and (ii) where did they occur and (c) who were involved in them;</li>

<li>(2) whether any specific discussions took place between departmental officials or principals and (a) parents and (b) pupils (i) before or (ii) after the incidents or clashes; if not, why not; if so,</li>

<li>(3) (a) when, (b) where, (c) who were involved and (d) what was the (i) nature and (ii) outcome of these discussions;</li>

<li>(4) whether his Department intends taking any action as a result of these incidents or clashes; if not, why not; if so, (a) what action and (b) when?</li>

</ol>

<p>B880E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) The principal of Crossroads No.<br/>3 Secondary was struck by a brick thrown by a pupil and his motor car was burnt out.<br/>The principal of Fezeka Secondary&#x2019;s motor car was damaged by stones thrown by pupils.<br/>Four motor cars of staff members of Intshukumo Comprehensive have been damaged by stones thrown by a group of pupils.</li>

<li>(ii) The causes have not yet been established beyond doubt.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) On 20 April 1989 at about 13h00, 13h30 and 13h55 respectively.</li>

<li>(ii) At I. D. Mkize Secondary, Fezeka Secondary and Intshukumo Comprehensive.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(c) The group of 50 persons at I. D. Mkize Secondary School consisted partly of pupils from I. D. Mkize Secondary and partly of outsiders.<br/>The group of seven at Fezeka Secondary consisted of pupils from I. D. Mkize.<br/>The group at Intshukumo Comprehensive also consisted of pupils from I. D. Mkize.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) No.</li>

<li>(ii) Parents have been informed of the incidents by letter.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) No. There were no previous indications of specific problems.</li>

<li>(ii) No. The principal has not yet returned to school. The acting principal was asked to point out to the pupils the seriousness of these transgressions.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3) (a), (b), (c) and (d) (i) and (ii) fall away.</li>

<li>(4) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) The pupil who threw the brick was identified and has been suspended.</li>

<li>(b) 28 April 1989.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>PE police district: offences reported</heading>

<question by="#malcomess" to="#minister_of_law_and_order">

<num>463.</num>

<from>Mr D J N MALCOMESS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Law and Order:</p>

<p>How many cases of (a) murder, (b) culpable homicide, (c) assault with intent to do grievous bodily harm, (d) common assault, (e) rape, (f) robbery, (g) theft of vehicles and cycles, (h) damage to property, (i) housebreaking with intent to steal and theft and (j) possession of drugs were reported at each specified police station in the Port Elizabeth police district in 1988?</p>

<writtenStatements>

<heading><span class="col_1321-1322" refersTo="page_0699"/>White/Coloured/Indian group areas proclaimed</heading>

<question by="#van_der_merwe" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>470.</num>

<from>Mr S S VAN DER MERWE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many group areas had been proclaimed in the Republic for (a) White, (b) Coloured and (c) Indian occupation as at 31 December 1988;</li>

<li>(2) what was the total area proclaimed for each group as at that date?</li>

</ol>

<p>B908E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#van_der_merwe">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) 579</li>

<li>(b) 533</li>

<li>(c) 255</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) 748 579,67 ha</li>

<li>(b) 104 117,41 ha</li>

<li>(c) 52 366,05 ha</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

<p>During 1988 1 602,73 ha of the White areas, 236,65 ha of the Coloured areas and 1,10 ha of the Indian areas were deproclaimed and left controlled. These totals have not been included in (2) above.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Open areas proclaimed</heading>

<question by="#van_der_merwe" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>471.</num>

<from>Mr S S VAN DER MERWE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<p>Whether any open areas were proclaimed in municipal areas in terms of section 19 of the Group Areas Act in 1988; if so, (a) how many, (b) in which municipal areas and (c) when?</p>

<p>B909E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#van_der_merwe">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>No open areas were proclaimed.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Urban areas for Black occupation</heading>

<question by="#walsh" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>473.</num>

<from>Mr J J WALSH</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<p>Whether any land was available in urban areas for Black occupation in 1988; if not, why not; if so, (a) how many hectares, and (b) where, in each case?</p>

<p>B911E</p>

<heading>Natural scientists: practising of unregisteredpersons</heading>

<p>asked the Minister of National Education:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether, in terms of Government Notice No R57 of 13 January 1989, he extended the exemption of all natural scientists employed in the Public Service from that section in the Natural Scientists Act, No 55 of 1982, which prohibits the practising as a natural scientist by unregistered persons; if so, when is it envisaged that this exemption will be lifted completely;</li>

<li>(2) whether there are reasons for exempting employees and officers in the Public Service from the application of section 20(1)(<i>b</i>) of the said Act; if so, what are these reasons;</li>

<li>(3) which organization, group or individual decided to seek exemption of members of the Public Service from the application of the abovementioned section;</li>

<li>(4) whether he will make a statement on the matter?</li>

</ol>

<p>B913E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_education" to="#burrows">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL EDUCATION:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.<br/>The present exemption is valid until 31 December 1989 when the matter will be reconsidered.</li>

<li>(2) Yes.<br/>Since the Natural Scientists&#x2019; Act, 1982 (Act 55 of 1982), was enacted, the importance of unrestricted competition for <span class="col_1331-1332" refersTo="page_0704"/>sound economic development has gained in prominence. The question then arose whether the prohibition of the practising of the natural sciences by unregistered persons who are not registered in terms of the aforesaid Act is in the public interest. For this reason the South African Council for Natural Scientists was requested to consider this matter in consultation with the Competition Board. The abovementioned exemption was granted against this background.</li>

<li>(3) The Commission for Administration directed the request at a time when the Department of National Education was already giving attention to the problem as set out above.</li>

<li>(4) No.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<heading>Public violence</heading>

<question by="#swart" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>485.</num>

<from>Mr R A F SWART</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) How many persons were charged with public violence in each specified magisterial district in 1988 and (b) what total number of the persons so charged were subsequently convicted;</li>

<li>(2) whether bail was granted to the accused in any of these cases; if not, why not; if so, in how many cases;</li>

<li><span class="col_1335-1336" refersTo="page_0706"/>(3) whether any charges of public violence were withdrawn; if so, how many;</li>

<li>(4) whether any of the persons so charged were under the age of 18 years; if so, how many in each case;</li>

<li>(5) whether these persons were prosecuted in terms of the provisions of the Children&#x2019;s Act, No 33 of 1960; if not, (a) why not and (b) who took the decision in this regard?</li>
