<writtenStatements>

<heading><span class="col_1339-1340" refersTo="page_0708"/>Randfontein area: telephone applications</heading>

<question by="#mulder" to="#minister_of_communications">

<num>490.</num>

<from>Dr C P MULDER</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Communications:&#x2020;</p>

<p>Whether there is any backlog in dealing with telephone applications in the Randfontein area at present; if so, (a) what (i) is the extent of and (ii) are the reasons for the backlog and (b) when is it anticipated that the backlog will be eliminated?</p>

<p>B933E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_communications" to="#mulder">

<from>The MINISTER OF COMMUNICATIONS:</from>

<p>Yes:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(a)</p></td>

<td><p>(b)</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(i)</p></td>

<td><p>(ii)</p></td>

<td><p></p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Randfontein exchange</p></td>

<td><p>13</p></td>

<td><p>a lack of spare cable leads</p></td>

<td><p>a cable project to provide the services is planned for the 1989/90 financial year. Alternative methods are at present being investigated to ascertain whether it would not be possible to meet the requirements of the applicants earlier. At this stage it is, however, not possible to indicate if these methods will be successful.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Wheatlands exchange</p></td>

<td><p>23</p></td>

<td><p>a shortage of telephone numbers and in some cases a lack of spare cable leads</p></td>

<td><p>if everything proceeds according to plan, the exchange will be extended by 888 lines during the first half of 1990 whereafter the requirements of 20 of the waiting applicants will be met. The premises of the remaining 3 applicants are situated within an area served by party lines. Although a special investigation is at present being undertaken, it is not possible at this early stage to give an indication as to when it will be possible to provide them with service.</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Central Cape Town area: facilities for therehabilitation of vagrants</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development">

<num>492.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of National Health and Population Development:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any facilities are available for the rehabilitation of vagrants of each race group from the central Cape Town area; if not, (a) why not and (b) what action is being taken in this regard; if so, (i) what facilities, (ii) (aa) where, (bb) when and (cc) by whom was each such facility provided and (iii) by whom are they financed;</li>

<li>(2) whether any additional facilities for the rehabilitation of such vagrants were provided during the past three years; if so, (a) what facilities, (b) (i) where, (ii) when and (iii) by whom were they provided, (c) by whom are they financed and (d) what total number of vagrants can be accommodated in these facilities;</li>

<li>(3) whether the State provides money for the purpose of handling the problem of vagrancy; if not, why not; if so, (a) to whom and (b) how much?</li>

</ol>

<p>B948E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL HEALTH AND POPULATION DEVELOPMENT:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes,

<ol>

<li>(a) and (b) fall away,

<ol>

<li><span class="col_1341-1342" refersTo="page_0709"/>(i) facilities for the rehabilitation of vagrants:

<ul><li>&#x2014; NICRO Night Shelter (all race groups),</li>

<li>&#x2014; Haven Night Shelter (all race groups),</li>

<li>&#x2014; Ark Mission (Whites),</li>

<li>&#x2014; The Carpenter&#x2019;s Shop (mostly Coloureds),</li>

</ul></li>

<li>(ii) (aa) (bb) and (cc)

<ul><li>&#x2014; NICRO Night Shelter is situated in Harrington Street, Cape Town and was established in 1980. The facility is provided by NICRO,</li>

<li>&#x2014; Haven Night Shelter is situated in Napier Street, Cape Town and was established in 1979. The facility is provided by the Catholic Welfare Bureau for Social Services,</li>

<li>&#x2014; Ark Mission is situated in Shortmarket Street, Cape Town and was established in 1980. The facility is provided by the Ark Mission,</li>

<li>&#x2014; The Carpenter&#x2019;s Shop is situated in Roeland Street, Cape Town and was established in 1981. The facility is provided by the organization, The Carpenter&#x2019;s Shop,</li>

</ul></li>

<li>(iii) each of these organizations manages their own financing;</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) no,</li>

<li>(3) no, sub-economic loans for the erection of hostels are available. If hostels comply with certain requirements subsidizing of the management thereof may be considered.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Venda: guarantees/sureties</heading>

<question by="#dalling" to="#minister_of_foreign_affairs">

<num>496.</num>

<from>Mr D J DALLING</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Foreign Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any guarantees or sureties were given directly or indirectly by the Government or any Department or agency of the Government to any person or organization for (a) loans granted, (b) lines of credit granted and (c) other specified financial services rendered to (i) the Government of, (ii) any Government Department of, (iii) a development corporation in and (iv) any other specified person or organization in Venda in the 1988-89 financial year; if so,</li>

<li>(2) (a) what amounts were involved in each case and (b) what was the total amount outstanding in terms of such guarantees or sureties as at the latest specified date for which information is available;</li>

<li>(3) whether foreign currencies are involved in any of these guarantees or sureties; if so, (a) what currencies, (b) how much is involved and (c) who is responsible for bearing the potential cost of exchange rate fluctuations?</li>

</ol>

<p>B957E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_foreign_affairs" to="#dalling">

<from>The MINISTER OF FOREIGN AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) No</li>

<li>(b) Yes, a guarantee in respect of overdraft facilities</li>

<li>(c) No</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) R45 million in respect of 1(b)</li>

<li>(b) Amount utilized not yet known in respect of 1(b)</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3) No

<ol>

<li>(a), (b) and (c) Fall away.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Ciskei: guarantees/sureties</heading>

<question by="#eglin" to="#minister_of_foreign_affairs">

<num>497.</num>

<from>Mr C W EGLIN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Foreign Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any guarantees or sureties were given directly or indirectly by the Government or any Department or agency of the Government to any person or organization for (a) loans granted, (b) lines of credit granted and (c) other specified financial services rendered to (i) the Government of, (ii) any Government Department of, (iii) a development corporation in and (iv) any other specified person or organization in Ciskei in the 1988-89 financial year; if so,</li>

<li><span class="col_1343-1344" refersTo="page_0710"/>(2) (a) what amounts were involved in each case and (b) what was the total amount outstanding in terms of such guarantees or sureties as at the latest specified date for which information is available;</li>

<li>(3) whether foreign currencies are involved in any of these guarantees or sureties; if so, (a) what currencies, (b) how much is involved and (c) who is responsible for bearing the potential cost of exchange rate fluctuations?</li>

</ol>

<p>B958E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_foreign_affairs" to="#eglin">

<from>The MINISTER OF FOREIGN AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) No</li>

<li>(b) Yes, a guarantee in respect of overdraft facilities</li>

<li>(c) No</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) R172 million in respect of (1)(b)</li>

<li>(b) Amount utilized not yet known in respect of (1)(b)</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3) No

<ol>

<li>(a), (b) and (c) Fall away.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Transkei: guarantees/sureties</heading>

<question by="#eglin" to="#minister_of_foreign_affairs">

<num>498.</num>

<from>Mr C W EGLIN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Foreign Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any guarantees or sureties were given directly or indirectly by the Government or any Department or agency of the Government to any person or organization for (a) loans granted, (b) lines of credit granted and (c) other specified financial services rendered to (i) the Government of, (ii) any Government Department of, (iii) a development corporation in and (iv) any other specified person or organization in Transkei in the 1988-89 financial year; if so,</li>

<li>(2) (a) what amounts were involved in each case and (b) what was the total amount outstanding in terms of such guarantees or sureties as at the latest specified date for which information is available;</li>

<li>(3) whether foreign currencies are involved in any of these guarantees or sureties; if so, (a) what currencies, (b) how much is involved and (c) who is responsible for bearing the potential cost of exchange rate fluctuations?</li>

</ol>

<p>B959E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_foreign_affairs" to="#eglin">

<from>The MINISTER OF FOREIGN AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) No</li>

<li>(b) Yes, a guarantee in respect of overdraft facilities</li>

<li>(c) No</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) R80 million in respect of (1)(b)</li>

<li>(b) Amount utilized not yet known in respect of (1)(b)</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3) No

<ol>

<li>(a), (b) and (c) Fall away.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Bophuthatswana: guarantees/sureties</heading>

<from>Mr C W EGLIN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Foreign Affairs:</p>

<li>(2) (a) what amounts were involved in each case and (b) what was the total amount outstanding in terms of such guarantees or sureties as at the latest specified date for which information is available;</li>

<li>(3) whether foreign currencies are involved in any of these guarantees or sureties; if so, (a) what currencies, (b) how much isinvolved and (c) who is responsible for bearing the potential cost of exchange rate fluctuations?</li>

<answer by="#minister_of_foreign_affairs" to="#eglin">

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Pollutants measured at urban locations</heading>

<question by="#lorimer" to="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development">

<num>506.</num>

<from>Mr R J LORIMER</from>

<p>asked the Minister of National Health and Population Development:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What was the latest average recorded atmospheric (a) lead level, (b) sulphuric acid level and (c) level of other specified significant pollutants measured at urban locations during (i) summer and (ii) winter at (aa) Cape Town City Hall, (bb) Port Elizabeth City Hall, (cc) Durban City Hall, (dd) Johannesburg City Hall and (ee) Munitoria, Pretoria;</li>

<li>(2) in respect of what specified date is this information furnished?</li>

</ol>

<p>B967E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development" to="#lorimer">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL HEALTH AND POPULATION DEVELOPMENT:</from>

<p>(1) (a) The figures for average lead concentration in the atmosphere are taken from a CSIR report which reported figures up to June 1988. The averages are therefore reported for the following time periods:</p>

<p>Summer: October 1987 to March 1988</p>

<p>Winter: April 1988 to June 1988</p>

<p>The results are reported in micrograms per cubic metre:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Summer</p></td>

<td><p>Winter</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(i) and (ii)</p></td>

<td><p>1987/88</p></td>

<td><p>1988</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(aa)</p></td>

<td><p>Cape Town City Hall</p></td>

<td><p>1,36</p></td>

<td><p>2,2</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(bb)</p></td>

<td><p>Port Elizabeth City Hall 0,86</p></td>

<td><p>1,55</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(cc)</p></td>

<td><p>Durban City Hall</p></td>

<td><p>0,52</p></td>

<td><p>1,17</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(dd)</p></td>

<td><p>Johannesburg City Hall</p></td>

<td><p>0,5</p></td>

<td><p>0,94</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(ee)</p></td>

<td><p>Munitoria Pretoria</p></td>

<td><p>0,6</p></td>

<td><p>1,67</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>The accepted maximum permissible concentrations for lead in the atmosphere is 2,5 micrograms per cubic metre (monthly average).</p>

<p>(b) Sulphuric acid levels in the air are not measured as such. Sulphuric acid formation is, however, related to the presence of sulphur dioxide. The following results expressed in micrograms per cubic metre are for sulphur dioxide concentrations. Some cities do not measure at the requested sites.</p>

<p>Summer: October 1987 to March 1988</p>

<p>Winter: April 1988 to September 1988</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Summer</p></td>

<td><p>Winter</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(i) and (ii)</p></td>

<td><p>1987/88</p></td>

<td><p>1988</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(aa) Cape Town (Drill Hall)</p></td>

<td><p>23,5</p></td>

<td><p>39,6</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(bb) Port Elizabeth (Central Area)</p></td>

<td><p>8</p></td>

<td><p>9</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(cc) Durban City Hall</p></td>

<td><p>21</p></td>

<td><p>36</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(dd) Johannesburg City Hall*</p></td>

<td><p>&#x2014;</p></td>

<td><p>&#x2014;</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(ee) Pretoria (Church Square)</p></td>

<td><p>9</p></td>

<td><p>25</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>* After measuring for a number of years, it was established that the sulphur dioxide levels in Johannesburg were extremely low and unlikely to change. Monitoring for sulphur dioxide was therefore discontinued.</p>

<p>The accepted maximum permissible concentration for sulphur dioxide is 80 micrograms per cubic metre (annual average).</p>

<p>(c) The following concentrations reflect the presence of particulate matter in the air. The results are expressed in micrograms per cubic metre.</p>

<p><i>Time periods:</i></p>

<p><i>Summer: October 1987 to March 1988</i></p>

<p><i>Winter: April 1988 to September 1988</i></p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Summer</p></td>

<td><p>Winter</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(i) and (ii)</p></td>

<td><p>1987/88</p></td>

<td><p>1988</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(aa) Cape Town (Drill Hall)</p></td>

<td><p>15</p></td>

<td><p>30</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(bb) Port Elizabeth (Central Area)</p></td>

<td><p>5</p></td>

<td><p>15</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(cc) Durban City Hall</p></td>

<td><p>15</p></td>

<td><p>40</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(dd) Johannesburg City Hall</p></td>

<td><p>25</p></td>

<td><p>55</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(ee) Pretoria (Church Square)</p></td>

<td><p>30</p></td>

<td><p>65</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>The accepted maximum permissible concentration for particulate matter is 100 micrograms per cubic metre (annual average).</p>

<p>At Cape Town City Hall nitrous oxides as well as ozone are also monitored. The average are expressed in micrograms per cubic metre.</p>

<p><i>Time periods:</i></p>

<p><span class="col_1349-1350" refersTo="page_0713"/><i>Summer: October 1987 to March 1988 Winter: April 1988 to September 1988</i></p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Summer</p></td>

<td><p>Winter</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>1987/88</p></td>

<td><p>1988</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Nitrous Oxides</p></td>

<td><p>140,6</p></td>

<td><p>233,6</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Ozone</p></td>

<td><p>15,6</p></td>

<td><p>11,16</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>The accepted maximum permissible concentration for these pollutants are as follows:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Nitrous Oxides</p></td>

<td><p>376 micrograms per cubic metre (annual average)</p></td>

<td><p>20 micrograms per cubic metre (annual average)</p></td>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Pilchards/anchovies: immature juveniles</heading>

<question by="#lorimer" to="#minister_of_environment_affairs">

<num>507.</num>

<from>Mr R J LORIMER</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Environment Affairs:</p>

<p>What estimated percentage of the catch of (a) pilchards and (b) anchovies in 1988 were immature juveniles?</p>

<p>B968E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_environment_affairs" to="#lorimer">

<from>The MINISTER OF ENVIRONMENT AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) 76&#x0025; by number, 27&#x0025; by mass.</li>

<li>(b) 52&#x0025; by number, 40&#x0025; by mass.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading><span class="col_1351-1352" refersTo="page_0714"/>Anchovy/pilchard landed</heading>

<question by="#lorimer" to="#minister_of_environment_affairs">

<num>508.</num>

<from>Mr R J LORIMER</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Environment Affairs;</p>

<p>How many tonnes of (a) anchovy and pilchard and (b) non-quota pelagic fish were landed during the 1988 fishing season?</p>

<p>B969E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_environment_affairs" to="#lorimer">

<from>The MINISTER OF ENVIRONMENT AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) 569 877 tonnes anchovy, 36 227 pilchard.</li>

<li>(b) 70 275 tonnes</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Indian/Coloured management committees</heading>

<question by="#malan" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>510.</num>

<from>Mr W C MALAN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he established any new (a) Coloured and (b) Indian management committees in 1988, if so, (i) why, (ii) in respect of which areas and (iii) how were these committees constituted;</li>

<li>(2) whether it is his intention to establish more (a) Coloured and (b) Indian management committees; if so, (i) why, (ii) in respect of which areas and (iii) how are these committees to be constituted?</li>

</ol>

<p>B971E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#malan">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>I do not establish these committees.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Tax lost</heading>

<question by="#schwarz" to="#minister_of_finance">

<num>512.</num>

<from>Mr H H SCHWARZ</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Finance:</p>

<p>What is the total amount of tax lost or expected to be lost as a result of tax concessions granted to decentralized or deconcentrated industries in respect of the year ended 31 March 1988?</p>

<p>B973E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_finance" to="#schwarz">

<from>The MINISTER OF FINANCE:</from>

<p>Because many taxpayers (especially companies) have not yet put in their 1988 returns, it is not possible to give a final figure, but on the basis of assessment already raised, the income tax concessions granted to decentralized or deconcentrated industries in respect of the year ending 31 March 1988 will amount to approximately R2,06 million.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Self-governing territories: Blacks employed</heading>

<question by="#soal" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>515.</num>

<from>Mr P G SOAL</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<p>How many Blacks in each self-governing territory were employed in undertakings established (a) on an agency basis and (b) by development corporations for such territories as as 31 December 1988?</p>

<p>B976E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#soal">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<p>As figures for 31 December 1988 are not available, figures as at 30 September 1988 are furnished.</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(a)</p></td>

<td><p>(b)</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>GaZankulu</p></td>

<td><p>6 700</p></td>

<td><p>4 716</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>KaNgwane</p></td>

<td><p>1 656</p></td>

<td><p>4 066</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>KwaNdebele</p></td>

<td><p>8 500</p></td>

<td><p>1 430</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>KwaZulu</p></td>

<td><p>36 881</p></td>

<td><p>7 470</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Lebowa</p></td>

<td><p>11 137</p></td>

<td><p>11 120</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Qwaqwa</p></td>

<td><p>21 500</p></td>

<td><p>4 543</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>The South African Development Trust Corporation Limited (STK) is responsible for the development of the Botshabelo industrial area. The number of Blacks employed in undertakings established on an agency basis in Botshabelo, amounted to 13 014 as at 30 September 1988.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading><span class="col_1365-1366" refersTo="page_0721"/>Death sentences</heading>

<question by="#swart" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>522.</num>

<from>Mr R A F SWART</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) How many persons were sentenced to death in 1988 and (b) in respect of the persons so sentenced, (i) how many were refused leave to appeal, (ii) how many sentences were altered or reduced by the Appellate Division, (iii) how many convictions were reversed by the Appellate Division, (iv) how many sentences were commuted by the State President, (v) how many were executed and (vi) how many were defended by <i>pro Deo</i> counsel at trial or appeal;</li>

<li>(2) whether consideration is being given to reviewing the grounds on which the death penalty may be imposed; if so, what are the relevant details?</li>

</ol>

<p>B983E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#swart">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) 213</li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) 118</li>

<li>(ii) 3</li>

<li>(iii) 1</li>

<li>(iv) 17</li>

<li>(v) 11</li>

<li>(vi) The information is not readily available. To obtain the information, it will mean that all the court records pertaining to such trials will have to be examined, which is not economically feasible.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) I refer the Honourable Member to my announcement during the recent debate on the Justice vote in this regard.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Persons awaiting execution</heading>

<question by="#swart" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>523.</num>

<from>Mr R A F SWART</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<p>How many (a) males and (b) females of each race group were awaiting execution in the Republic as at the latest specified date for which information is available?</p>

<p>B984E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#swart">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<p>On 18 May 1989 7 persons (5 Blackmen and 2 Whitemen) were awaiting execution. Theundermentioned (a) males and (b) femaleswere in detention on that date after havingbeen sentenced to death:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>(a)</p></td>

<td><p>Black</p></td>

<td><p>227</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Coloured</p></td>

<td><p>40</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>White</p></td>

<td><p>13</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Indian</p></td>

<td><p>5</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(b)</p></td>

<td><p>Black</p></td>

<td><p>1</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Coloured</p></td>

<td><p>1</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>All the cases are still in various stages of the post sentence process. In most of the cases appeals are pending.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Non-South African citizens residing permanently in SA</heading>

<question by="#van_der_merwe" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>524.</num>

<from>Mr S S VAN DER MERWE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) How many (i) adults and (ii) children who are not South African citizens are at present permanently resident in South Africa and (b) in respect of what date is this information furnished;</li>

<li>(2) (a) what are the countries of which such (i) adults and (ii) children are citizens and (b) how many of these (i) adults and (ii) children are citizens of each such country?</li>

</ol>

<p>B985E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#van_der_merwe">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<heading>Nurses: applications not accepted</heading>

<question by="#ellis" to="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development">

<num>530.</num>

<from>Mr M J ELLIS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of National Health and Population Development:</p>

<p>Whether any applications to train as nurses at institutions for the training of (a) White, (b) Coloured, (c) Indian and (d) Black nurses were not accepted in 1988; if so, (i) how many, and (ii) why, in each case?</p>

<p>B992E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_health_and_population_development" to="#ellis">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL HEALTH AND POPULATION DEVELOPMENT:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a), (b) and (d) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(i) 35 449</li>

<li><span class="col_1369-1370" refersTo="page_0723"/>(ii) Reasons in respect of all population groups are as follows:

<ul><li>&#x2014; unsuitable candidates for example the lack of educational qualifications,</li>

<li>&#x2014; candidates apply to more than one training centre increasing the number of applications,</li>

<li>&#x2014; candidates accepted but do not turn up for training,</li>

<li>&#x2014; shortage of posts for black students in some areas,</li>

<li>&#x2014; shortage of clinical facilities,</li>

<li>&#x2014; funds for salaries and infrastructure insufficient,</li>

</ul></li>

</ol></li>

<li>(c) no.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>SATS: amount allocated</heading>

<question by="#cronj&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_transport_affairs">

<num>534.</num>

<from>Mr P C CRONJ&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Transport Affairs:</p>

<p>What total amount was allocated by the South African Transport Services for the 1989-90 financial year for the payment of staff housing subsidies on a (a) compulsory and (b) voluntary basis?</p>

<p>B996E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_transport_affairs" to="#cronj&#x00E9;">

<from>The MINISTER OF TRANSPORT AFFAIRS:</from>

<p>(a) and (b) Transport Services does not pay housing subsidies to its personnel but has housing schemes which entails housing loans at low interest rates. Transport Services&#x2019; contribution in respect of the difference in interest rates for the 1989/90 financial year amounts to R157 million (estimated and subject to the number of loans taken up and/or redeemed).</p>

<p>Departmental houses are rented at a nominal rent to Transport Services personnel. The exceeding of expenditure over income in respect of departmental housing for the 1989/90 financial year is estimated at R134 million.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Assistance in respect of motor-car ownership</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>538.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<p>Whether any officials of the Cape Region of the Department of Education and Training receive assistance in respect of motor-car ownership; if so, (a) what is the nature of the assistance, (b) what ranks of officials qualify, (c) how many officials are involved and (d) in respect of what date is this information furnished?</p>

<p>B1000E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) Motor Financing Scheme for Senior Officials.</li>

<li>(ii) Subsidised Motor Transport.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) Motor Financing Scheme for Senior Officials: Regional Chief Director.</li>

<li>(ii) Subsidised Motor Transport: Vehicles are not allocated according to rank, but on account of the number of days per month that the vehicle will be officially used, the distance in kilometres that will be covered monthly and the availability of funds.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(c)

<ol>

<li>(i) Motor Financing Scheme for Senior Officials: 1</li>

<li>(ii) Subsidised Motor Transport: 14</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(d) 15 May 1989.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Greater Soweto: population</heading>

<question by="#walsh" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>541.</num>

<from>Mr J J WALSH</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<p>What was the (a) adult male, (b) adult female (c) child population of Greater Soweto as at 31 December 1988?</p>

<p>B1006E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#walsh">

<from><span class="col_1375-1376" refersTo="page_0726"/>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrator of Transvaal and he has furnished the following information:</p>

<p>The statistics given are based on information from the 1985 population census, report no 02-85-09. The growth in population is calculated at the acknowledged growt rate of 2,39&#x0025; per annum.</p>

<ol>

<li>(a) 264 336</li>

<li>(b) 253 244</li>

<li>(c) 334 486</li>

</ol>

<p>852 066</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Eastern Cape, sportsfields: certain contracts for grassing</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>542.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether, with reference to his reply to Question No 8 on 21 March 1989, the person to whom certain contracts were awarded for establishing or grassing sportsfields in the Eastern Cape is in any way associated with a certain business concern, the name of which has been furnished to the Minister&#x2019;s Department for the purpose of his reply; if so, what is the name of this (a) person and (b) business concern;</li>

<li>(2) whether this person or business concern or any other business concerns with which this person is associated were awarded any contracts to do work at certain schools, the names of which have also been furnished to the Minister&#x2019;s Department, over the past five years; if not, which business concerns were awarded contracts for this type of work at these schools; if so, (a) what contracts, (b) what was the total amount of money involved and (c) why were these contracts not mentioned in the reply to Question No 8 on 21 March 1988;</li>

<li>(3) whether normal tender procedures were followed in respect of these contracts; if not, (a) why not and (b) what procedures were followed?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1001E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) Wally Holmes.</li>

<li>(b) Grassmaster.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) No.

<ol>

<li>(a), (b) and (c) fall away.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3) At the schools, of which the names have been furnished to the Department, no work was done to sportsfields over the past five years.

<ol>

<li>(a) and (b) fall away.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:&#x2020;</p>

<p>(a) What is the total cost of the construction of the reform school for Black juveniles near Kinross, (b) (i) at what price and (ii) on what date was the land for this school purchased, (c) what are the operating costs per month or per annum and (d) (i) how many pupils (aa) are and (bb) can be accommodated in this school and (ii) in respect of what date is this information furnished?</p>

<p>B1010E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#de_jager">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) The estimated final cost after completion of the renovations and alterations to the existing building as well as the addition of new buildings is R9 736 486</li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) R1 276 000</li>

<li>(ii) 30 November 1987</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(c) R1 325 000 per annum</li>

<li>(d)

<ol>

<li>(i)

<ol>

<li>(aa) 136</li>

<li>(bb) 360</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(ii) 12 May 1989.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Farms acquired from Verco (Pty) Ltd: gamecount</heading>

<question by="#soal" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>546.</num>

<from>Mr P G SOAL</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<p>Whether, with reference to his reply to Question No 221 on 24 April 1989, (a) his Department or (b) the South African Development Trust undertook a game count on the farms acquired from Verco (Pty) Limited; if so, what was the result of the game court; if not, why not?</p>

<p>B1003E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#soal">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) No.</li>

<li><span class="col_1379-1380" refersTo="page_0728"/>(b) Yes. A game count was undertaken by the South African Development Trust in collaboration with the Transvaal Provincial Administration (Nature Conservation). The game count revealed that there were less game on the property than that claimed by the previous owner.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Certain person: permission to reside inWoodmead East</heading>

<question by="#dalling" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>549.</num>

<from>Mr D J DALLING</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether, with reference to information furnished to the Minister&#x2019;s Department for the purpose of his reply, an application was received from a certain person for permission for him and his wife and family to reside in the suburb of Woodmead East, Sandton; if so, (a) when was this application received, (b) what address was given by the applicant as his present residential address, (c) what grounds were stated by the applicant in support of his application, (d) (i) what statement was made in paragraph 15 of the application form in support of this application and (ii) to whom was this statement made and (e) what are the (i) names and (ii) titles of the applicant and his wife;</li>

<li>(2) whether any objections to this application were recived; if so, (a) from whom and (b) on what grounds;</li>

<li>(3) whether a decision has been reached on this application; if so, (a) what was the decision and (b) what were the reasons for arriving at this decision?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1019E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#dalling">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrator of Transvaal and he has furnished to the following information:</p>

<p>(1), (2), and (3) The decision to refuse the application of the persons whose names have been furnished for the purpose of this reply, was taken after due consideration of all the relevant facts.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>East London, certain coporation: money paid</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_manpower">

<num>550.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Manpower:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether his Department paid any money to a certain corporation in East London, the name of which has been furnished to the Minister&#x2019;s Department for the purpose of his reply, in terms of any job creation or other scheme; if so, (a) why, (b) how much, (c) when, (d) on whose authority, (e) for what purpose and (f) what is the name of this corporation;</li>

<li>(2) whether this money was to be used directly or indirectly on any school-building projects; if not, for what projects was the money used; if so, for what specified school projects;</li>

<li>(3) whether the proposed projects were completed; if so, when; if not, (a) why not, (b) which projects (i) were and (ii) were not completed, (c) how much money has been recovered and (d) what action has been taken in this regard;</li>

<li>(4) whether he will furnish the names of the (a) directors and (b) owners of this corporation; if not, why not; if so, what are their names?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1031E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#acting_minister_of_manpower" to="#andrew">

<from>The ACTING MINISTER OF MANPOWER:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes;

<ol>

<li>(a) For job creation.</li>

<li>(b) R1 million.</li>

<li>(c) 1985.</li>

<li>(d) The Committee for Job Creation.</li>

<li>(e) For a housing and temporary school project.</li>

<li>(f) Duncan Village Corporation.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Yes, for a housing and temporary school project.</li>

<li>(3) No.

<ol>

<li>(a) The project did not progress satisfactorily.</li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) 64 units were completed.</li>

<li>(ii) 104 units were not proceeded with.</li>

</ol></li>

<li><span class="col_1381-1382" refersTo="page_0729"/>(c) Payment was finalised on an auditor&#x2019;s certificate for work completed. Final payment was only for work completed.</li>

<li>(d) Falls away.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(4) (a) and (b) The contract was entered into with A Lightbody and P de Pontes on behalf of Duncan Village Corporation.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Schools: provision of stationery</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>554.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether schools falling under his Department are provided with (a) paper and ink for producing question papers and (b) examination pads for pupils in respect of June and September examinations; if not, (i) why not, (ii) what supplies are provided for these purposes and (iii) on what basis is the provision of such supplies calculated; if so, (aa) when, (bb) on what basis, and (cc) what supplies are provided, in each case;</li>

<li>(2) whether this applies to both secondary and primary schools; if not, what are the relevant details?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1041E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Yes.</li>

<li>(b) No.

<ol>

<li>(i) A sufficient quantity of stationery is issued to pupils at the beginning of every year.</li>

<li>(ii) Stationery (also for examination purposes) as required per subject/per standard is issued.</li>

<li>(iii) The Department annually determines pupils&#x2019; annual stationery requirements for the different standards and stationery is supplied in accordance therewith. (aa), (bb) and (cc) fall away.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Yes.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_education" to="#burrows">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL EDUCATION:</from>

<p>(1) The pro visional figure for the expenditure per full time equivalent pupil/student for the CS Sector for 1988/89, excluding the remuneration of CS educators, for each of the four departments of state responsible for education, is as follows:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Education and Culture, Administration: House of Assembly</p></td>

<td><p>&#x2014; R1 308</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Education and Culture, Administration: House of Representatives</p></td>

<p>These figures are strongly influenced by a number of factors falling outside the control of the state, such as for example population migration, and the higher cost of education associated with the senior secondary school phase.</p>

<p>(2) Yes, as and when the economic situation in the country improves.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading><span class="col_1383-1384" refersTo="page_0730"/>Privatisation of inter-city luxury coach services ofSATS</heading>

<question by="#cronj&#x00E9;" to="#minister_of_transport_affairs">

<num>558.</num>

<from>Mr P C CRONJ&#x00C9;</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Transport Affairs:</p>

<p>Whether any further steps have been taken regarding the privatisation of the inter-city luxury coach services of the South African Transport Services since his reply to Question No 659 on 22 March 1988; if not, why not; if so, what steps?</p>

<p>B1046E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_transport_affairs" to="#cronj&#x00E9;">

<from>The MINISTER OF TRANSPORT AFFAIRS:</from>

<p>Yes. As indicated in my reply to Question No 659 of 22 March 1988 all passenger bus services and not only the inter-city luxury bus services will be included in the privatisation action.</p>

<p>As a first step to privatisation the bus passenger service is presently operated as a separate business unit of the Business Undertaking Road Transport. Active steps to place the service on a profitable basis are presently being undertaken. It is anticipated that the profit potential will be realised within three years whereafter privatisation actions can be proceeded with.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Joint management centres</heading>

<question by="#de_beer" to="#state_president">

<num>563.</num>

<from>Dr Z J DE BEER</from>

<p>asked the State President:</p>

<p>(a) How many joint management centres had been established as at 31 December 1988, (b) where are these centres located, (c) who (i) is the chairman and (ii) are the members of each of these centres and (d) what tasks do these members perform in respect of these joint management centres?</p>

<p>B1052E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#state_president" to="#de_beer">

<from>The STATE PRESIDENT:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a) 11.</li>

<li>(b) Cape Town, Port Elizabeth, Durban, Bloemfontein, Johannesburg, Potchefstroom, Kimberley, Pietersburg, Nelspruit, Pretoria and Walvis Bay.</li>

<li>(c)

<ol>

<li>(i) Officials of state departments and provinces and officers of the security forces. Chairmen are elected annually.</li>

<li>(ii) Chairmen and members of joint management centres vary continually as and when officials are transferred.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(d) Members of the JMCs co-ordinate and synchronise the actions of their respective departments and privinces in line function context.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<heading>State/private children&#x2019;s homes for Black children</heading>

<question by="#ellis" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>569.</num>

<from>Mr M J ELLIS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) How many (i) State and (ii) privately administered children&#x2019;s homes were there for Black children in the Republic as at the latest specified date for which figures are available and (b) how many children were accommodated in these homes as at that date;</li>

<li>(2) whether there is a shortage of such accommodation for Black children; is so, (a) what is the nature of the shortage and (b) what steps are to be taken to overcome such shortage?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1060E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#ellis">

<from><span class="col_1387-1388" refersTo="page_0732"/>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrators of the difference provinces and they have furnished the following information:</p>

<p>Transvaal:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) None as at 31 January 1989</li>

<li>(ii) 5</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) 400</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) Accommodation.</li>

<li>(b) A State Children&#x2019;s Home which will accommodate 200 children, is being erected and will be completed during the present financial year.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

<p>Orange Free State:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) None.</li>

<li>(ii) None.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) Falls away</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) There is a shortage of children&#x2019;s homes in this province as no such homes exist and the provincial government is dependent on outside instances.</li>

<li>(b) Approval in principle has already been given for the change-over of a portion of the Tshireletsong Provincial Place of Safety for Black children to a children&#x2019;s home to house 85 children up to the age of eighteen years. The first children will be admitted shortly.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

<p>Natal:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) None.</li>

<li>(ii) 5 on 15 March 1989.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) 505.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) Housing for normal boys (138) and girls (50) aged 12 to 18 years and a separate institution for mentally and physically handicapped children (100) of all ages.</li>

<li>(b) Private organisations are encouraged to extend existing institutions and to develop new institutions. Institutions mentioned in (2) (a) have been included in a list of priorities for the allocation of additional funds.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

<p>Cape Province:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) None.</li>

<li>(ii) 2 on 31 March 1989<br/>

Masikhule Children&#x2019;s Home, Phillipi &#x2014; Registered for 11 children. Opened: 1 February 1989<br/>

Khayamnandi Children&#x2019;s<br/>

Home, Langa &#x2014; Registered for 25 children. Opened: 1988.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) Masikhule Children&#x2019;s Home: 6<br/>

Khayamnandi Children&#x2019;s Home: 24</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) For cases requiring long-term accommodation.</li>

<li>(b) 3 Children&#x2019;s Homes in planning phase:<br/>

State Children&#x2019;s Home: Northern Cape &#x2014; (Kimberley) for 80 children. Date of opening: 1/10/1991.<br/>

State Children&#x2019;s Home: Eastern Cape &#x2014; (Port Elizabeth) for 120 children. Date of opening: 1/10/1989.<br/>

State Children&#x2019;s Home: Western Cape &#x2014; (Khayelitsha) for 100 children. Date of opening: 1/10/1991.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Activities undertaken by the State: privatisation</heading>

<question by="#lorimer" to="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation">

<num>573.</num>

<from>Mr R J LORIMER</from>

<p>asked the Minister for Administration and Privatisation:</p>

<p>Whether any activities previously or currently undertaken by the State were privatised in 1988 or are planned to be privatised in 1989; if not, why not; if so, in respect of those activities (a) already privatised and (b) planned to be privatised, (i) what undertakings are involved and (ii) what is the estimated saving in (aa) State expenditure and (bb) manpower employed by the State?</p>

<p>B1065E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation" to="#lorimer">

<from>The MINISTER FOR ADMINISTRATION AND PRIVATISATION:</from>

<td><p>(ii)</p></td>

<td><p></p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Contracting out of the planning design and construction of &#x2014;</p></td>

<td><p>Budget of &#x2014;</p></td>

</tr>

<td><p>R 400 million</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>The regulation of meat standards and the roller marking of meat carcasses</p></td>

<td><p>Savings in &#x2014;</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(aa) State expenditure: R2 4 million.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>This activity was transferred to the Meat Board on 1 December 1988.</p></td>

<td><p>(bb) Manpower: 160 staff members.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p><span class="col_1391-1392" refersTo="page_0734"/>(4) Transvaal Provincial Administration:</p></td>

<td><p></p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Engraving of medals.</p></td>

<td><p>Savings in &#x2014;</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<tr>

<td colspan="2"><p>(b) With reference to the Public Service as well as other government institutions, it is planned to privatise the following activities in 1989:</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(i)</p></td>

<td><p>(ii)</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>Undertakings involved</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(1)</p></td>

<td><p>The SA Iron and Steel Industrial Corporation, Limited</p></td>

<td><p>None due to existing autonomy of Iscor.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(2)</p></td>

<td><p>Public Service wide:</p>

<p>The following services will be phased out/ privatised over a period of three years from 1 April 1989:</p>

<p>(a) Catering</p>

<p>(b) Cleaning Services</p>

<p>(c) Horticultural Services.</p>

<p>(d) Nurseries.</p></td>

<td><p>Presently a budget of R360 million and approximately 38 000 employees are involved with these services.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(3)</p></td>

<td><p>Building and maintenance of roads:</p>

<p>From 1 April 1989 to 1994/&#x2019;95 a level of contracting out of 75&#x0025; must be reached by the departments/administrations and other road authorities involved.</p></td>

<td><p>A budget of R2 200 million and 32 000 employees are presently involved with this activity.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(4)</p></td>

<td><p>Sorghum Beer Industry</p>

<p>The sale of the sorghum beer undertakings of the former Development Boards.</p></td>

<td><p>The breweries had a turnover of R500 million in 1988 and 3 439 staff members are involved.</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>(5)</p></td>

<td><p>Department of Justice:</p>

<p>The serving of criminal process documents in particular cases.</p></td>

<td><p>No savings will be realised. The job is at present being done by policemen together with their other duties.</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>Apart from the above-mentioned activities several other activities are being investigated with a view to privatisation.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<heading>Applications for deregulation</heading>

<question by="#lorimer" to="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation">

<num>574.</num>

<from>Mr R J LORIMER</from>

<p>asked the Minister for Administration and Privatisation:</p>

<p>(a) How many applications for deregulation had been (i) received, (ii) investigated and (iii) granted in terms of the Temporary Removal of Restrictions on Economic Activities Act, No 87 of 1986, and (b) in how many cases had deregulation taken place, as at 31 December 1988?</p>

<p>B1066E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_for_administration_and_privatisation" to="#lorimer">

<from><span class="col_1393-1394" refersTo="page_0735"/>The MINISTER FOR ADMINISTRATION AND PRIVATISATION:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) Twelve</li>

<li>(ii) Twelve</li>

<li>(iii) One</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) Miscellaneous actions on deregulation which, on account of the nature and extent thereof make it impossible to quantify as specific cases, already took place up to and including December 1988. Since then further applications referring to approximately 1 100 small businesses were granted.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Zaire: planning/provision of private zoo</heading>

<question by="#burrows" to="#minister_of_national_education">

<num>574.</num>

<from>Mr R M BURROWS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of National Education:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the National Zoological Gardens or his Department is involved in the planning or provision of a private zoo for the President of Zaire; if so, (a) by whom was this involvement authorized and (b) what is it estimated will be the total cost to his Department in this regard;</li>

<li>(2) whether his Department will be reimbursed by Zaire for services rendered and equipment provided; if not, why not; if so, (a) in what way, (b) what Services are intended to be provided in the construction phase of this zoo and (c) from what source will funds be obtained for expenditure incurred by South Africa in connection with the construction of the zoo?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1043E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_national_education" to="#burrows">

<from>The MINISTER OF NATIONAL EDUCATION:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes, the National Zoological Gardens is involved, but only in providing advice in respect of the planning for a proposed zoo for the President of Zaire.

<ol>

<li>(a) The Council of the National Zoological Gardens.</li>

<li>(b) Nothing.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Falls away.

<ol>

<li>(a), (b) and (c) Falls away.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<ol>

<li>(a) R4 891 944,</li>

<li>(b) R3 232 069,</li>

<li>(c) R4 409 308.<br/>Please note that the amounts mentioned above were paid in respect of films released during the 1987-88 financial year as well as films released during previous years, but which still qualified for subsidies in that year.<br/>Also take note that the Department of Trade and Industries administered the subsidy scheme during the 1987-88 financial year.</li>

<li><span class="col_1395-1396" refersTo="page_0736"/>(2) in respect of the above-mentioned firearms, (a) how many were (i) lost, (ii) stolen and (iii) recovered in 1988 and (b) (i) on how many occasions is it estimated were they used subsequent to being so lost or stolen and (ii) how many persons were (aa) injured, (bb) seriously injured and (cc) killed as a result?</li>

<p>B1067E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#malan">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>It is not considered to be in the public interest to provide this information.</p>

</answer>

<heading>Members of community councils/Black localauthorities: killed/injured</heading>

<question by="#malan" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>577.</num>

<from>Mr W C MALAN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether any (a) community councillors and (b) members of Black local authorities were attacked and (i) killed or (ii) injured in 1988; if so, how many in each case;</li>

<li>(2) whether any of these (a) community councillors and (b) members of Black local authorities were attacked and (i) killed or (ii) injured as a result of their holding these offices; if so, how many in each case?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1068E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#malan">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrators of the different provinces and they have furnished the following information:</p>

<p>Orange Free State</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) No. Rest of question falls away.</li>

</ol>

<p>Natal</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) No. Rest of question falls away.</li>

</ol>

<p>Cape Province</p>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Not applicable &#x2014; Community Councils were replaced by Town Committees on 11 July 1986 (GK 1480 of 11 July 1986).</li>

<li>(b) Yes

<ol>

<li>(i) one</li>

<li>(ii) one</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) Not applicable</li>

<li>(b) Unknown</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

<p>Transvaal</p>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Falls away, because in 1988 community councils no longer existed.</li>

<li>(b) Yes (i) None (ii) Four</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) Falls away</li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) Falls away</li>

<li>(ii) The reason for the attacks in which councillors were injured are not known.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<heading>Telephone services: concessions</heading>

<question by="#schwarz" to="#minister_of_communications">

<num>582.</num>

<from>Mr H H SCHWARZ</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Communications:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether his Department grants any concessions to (a) social pensioners and (b) persons over any specific age in respect of the use of telephone services; if not, why not; if so, (i) what concessions in each case and (ii) what are the age limits involved;</li>

<li>(2) whether he intends improving these concessions; if not, why not; if so, what are the relevant details?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1072E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_communications" to="#schwarz">

<from>The MINISTER OF COMMUNICATIONS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Yes, they pay only R25,00 instead of the normal prescribed fee of R140,00 for the installation or transfer of a telephone service;</li>

<li>(b) no;</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) no, as current installation/transfer fees as well as the monthly rental still fall far short of that which is required to make the provision of telephone service cost-related, whilst in the case of call charges, it would be impossible to prevent misuse by the subscribers&#x2019; relatives or friends. Furthermore, the granting of concenssions with regard to rental and call charges to pensioners and the aged would no doubt elicit representations from other groups or persons which would have to be turned down regard being had to the financial implications involved.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<td><p>1/7/87-30/6/88</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Whites</p></td>

<td><p>259</p></td>

<td><p>222</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Coloureds</p></td>

<td><p>1 808</p></td>

<td><p>1 998</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Indians</p></td>

<td><p>35</p></td>

<td><p>38</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Blacks</p></td>

<td><p>2 516</p></td>

<td><p>3 271</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Total</p></td>

</tr>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Black workers/persons repatriated</heading>

<question by="#van_der_merwe" to="#minister_of_home_afairs">

<num>590.</num>

<from>Mr S S VAN DER MERWE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Afairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many Black (a) workers and (b) persons from Zimbabwe, Lesotho, Swaziland, Botswana and Mozambique, respectively, were repatriated in 1988;</li>

<li><span class="col_1401-1402" refersTo="page_0739"/>(2) how many Black (a) workers and (b) persons from each of the above countries had been granted exemption from repatriation on the ground of long service as at the latest specified date for which figures are available?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1082E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#van_der_merwe">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<p>(1) Separate statistics are not kept in respect of Black workers removed. However, the total number of Black persons removed in 1988 to the countries in question is as follows:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Zimbabwe</p></td>

<td><p>3 527</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Lesotho</p></td>

<td><p>4 400</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Swaziland</p></td>

<td><p>1 839</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Botswana</p></td>

<td><p>757</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Mozambique</p></td>

<td><p>33 446</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>(2) During the period 1 January 1988 up to 30 April 1989, the following exemptions in terms of section 7 <i>bis</i> (1) of the Aliens Act, 1937 (Act 1 of 1937) were granted in respect of workers and persons from the following countries:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p></p></td>

<td><p>(a)</p></td>

<td><p>(b)*</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Zimbabwe</p></td>

<td><p>158</p></td>

<td><p>5</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Lesotho</p></td>

<td><p>50</p></td>

<td><p>52</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Swaziland</p></td>

<td><p>6</p></td>

<td><p>14</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Botswana</p></td>

<td><p>12</p></td>

<td><p>8</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Mozambique</p></td>

<td><p>114</p></td>

<td><p>21</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<p>Separate statistics of exemptions granted to persons on the ground of long service alone, are not available for the period in question.</p>

<p>*Excluding workers, but including the wives and children of workers.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Applications for South African travel documents</heading>

<question by="#van_der_merwe" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>591.</num>

<from>Mr S S VAN DER MERWE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many citizens of each specified independent Black state (a) had applied for and (b) had been granted South African travel documents since the independence of each of these countries up to the latest specified date for which figures are available;</li>

<li>(2) whether any applications were not granted; if so, (a) in respect of which such states and (b) why in each case?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1083E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#van_der_merwe">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) and (b) Separate statistics in respect of these states are not kept. Since the dates of independence of the TBVC countries until 31 December 1988, 24 122 applications were received, of which 24 086 were approved.</li>

<li>(2) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) Separate statistics are not kept in respect of each state.</li>

<li>(b) It is not policy to disclose reasons for refusal of applications.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Deportation of citizens of specified areas</heading>

<question by="#van_der_merwe" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>592.</num>

<from>Mr S S VAN DER MERWE</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:</p>

<p>(a) How many citizens of (i) Ciskei, (ii) Transkei, (iii) Venda and (iv) Bophuthatswana were deported from the Republic in 1988 and (b) in terms of what statutory provisions were they deported in each case?</p>

<p>B1084E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#van_der_merwe">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) 7</li>

<li>(ii) 68</li>

<li>(iii) 6</li>

<li>(iv) 4</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) In terms of section 43 of the Admission of Persons to the Republic Regulation Act, 1972 (Act 59 of 1972).</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Black housing: estimated shortage</heading>

<question by="#walsh" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>596.</num>

<from>Mr J J WALSH</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) What was the estimated shortage of housing for Blacks outside the self-governing territories as at 31 December 1988;</li>

<li>(2) how many houses were provided in 1988 for such Blacks in each province by (a) the State, (b) local authorities and (c) the private sector;</li>

<li>(3) when is it anticipated that the above-mentioned shortage will be eliminated?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1088E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#walsh">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrators of the different provinces and they have furnished the following information:</p>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>(1) Orange Free State</p></td>

<td><p>= 66 920</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Natal</p></td>

<td><p>= 33 295</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Transvaal</p></td>

<td><p>= 343 271</p></td>

</tr>

<tr>

<td><p>Cape Province</p></td>

<td><p>= 133 688</p></td>

</tr>

</table>

<ol>

<li>(2) The hon member is referred to my answer to Question 228.</li>

<li>(3) It will depend on the effect of urbanisation, the involvement of the private sector and the availability of funds.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#walsh">

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Gill nets/loads of tuna: representations</heading>

<question by="#eglin" to="#minister_of_environment_affairs">

<num>599.</num>

<from>Mr C W EGLIN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Environment Affairs:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether he has received any representations in connection with the off-loading of exceptionally long lengths of gill nets and loads of tuna by fishing vessels at Cape Town Harbour; if so, (a) what was the purport of each such representation and (b) from whom was each received;</li>

<li>(2) whether he has investigated any of the matters raised in such representations; if not, why not; if so, (a) which matters were investigated and (b) with what results in each case;</li>

<li>(3) whether he has taken any action as a result of these investigations; if not, why not; if so, what action?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1103E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_environment_affairs" to="#eglin">

<from><span class="col_1411-1412" refersTo="page_0744"/>The MINISTER OF ENVIRONMENT AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) Representation to ban the offloading of tuna caught with gill-nets, in South African ports.</li>

<li>(b) Mrs Nan Rice, Secretary, Dolphin Action and Protection Group, Fish Hoek.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) The whole matter is being investigated.</li>

<li>(b) The Department of Environment Affairs is investigating the possibility of drafting legislation.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3) The Department is investigating the possibility of drawing up regulations forbidding the off-loading in SA ports of any tuna caught with the gill-net method.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Langebaan: permits for premises for Black/Indian/Coloured persons</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning">

<num>600.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Constitutional Development and Planning:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether a certain organization in Langebaan, the name of which has been furnished to the Minister&#x2019;s Department for the purpose of his reply, has made any requests to his Department for permits or permission to allow (a) Coloured, (b) Indian and (c) Black persons to (i) purchase and (ii) occupy premises there; if so, (aa) what permits or permission was requested, (bb) what was his response, (cc) what were the reasons for this response and (dd) what is the name of this organization;</li>

<li>(2) whether any permits or permission is required in this regard; if so, why?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1104E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_constitutional_development_and_planning" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF CONSTITUTIONAL DEVELOPMENT AND PLANNING:</from>

<p>This matter vests in the Administrator of the Cape Province and he has furnished the following information:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) No.</li>

<li>(2) Yes, in terms of the Group Areas Act, 1966.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Vaalwater, Waterberg: employment of illegalimmigrants</heading>

<question by="#langley" to="#minister_of_home_affairs">

<num>601.</num>

<from>Mr T LANGLEY</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Home Affairs:&#x2020;</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether a certain farmer near Vaalwater in the Waterberg district, whose name has been furnished to the Minister&#x2019;s Department for the purpose of his reply, recently committed certain offences with regard to the employment of illegal immigrants from neighbouring states; if so, (a) what was the nature of these offences, (b) how many immigrants were involved and (c) what is the name of the farmer;</li>

<li>(2) whether his Department has taken any legal action against this farmer; if not, why not; if so, what are the details of this action?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1106E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_home_affairs" to="#langley">

<from>The MINISTER OF HOME AFFAIRS:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) Yes.

<ol>

<li>(a) Contravening the provisions of the Aliens Act, 1937 (Act 1 of 1937)</li>

<li>(b) 51</li>

<li>(c) Mr J P Lauryssen</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) Yes. A criminal charge has been laid against Mr Lauryssen in respect of which the State Prosecutor of the Magistrate&#x2019;s Court, Nylstroom, issued a summons for Mr Lauryssen to appear in Court.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Iketlo Primary School</heading>

<question by="#andrew" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>602.</num>

<from>Mr K M ANDREW</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) Whether the Iketlo Primary School in Guguletu has (a) toilet facilities for its pupils, (b) running water and (c) electricity; if so, (i) what are the details of the facilities provided, (ii) when were they provided and (iii) who provided them; if not, (aa) why not and (bb) when is it anticipated that such facilities will be provided;</li>

<li>(2) (a) how many pupils are currently registered at this school and (b) in respect of what date is this information furnished;</li>

<li>(3) whether there are any sports facilities for these pupils; if so, what are the details of the facilities provided; if not, (a) why not <span class="col_1413-1414" refersTo="page_0745"/>and (b) when is it anticipated that such facilities will be provided;</li>

<li>(4) whether his Department owns any land adjacent to the Iketlo Primary School; if so, (a) what is the extent of this land and (b) what is his intention with regard to such land?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1107E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#andrew">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a), (b) and (c) No (i), (ii) and (iii) fall away.

<ol>

<li>(aa) Iketlo Primary School at present makes use of the adjacent Litha Primary School&#x2019;s toilets and water supply. This school is situated on the same site as Iketlo.</li>

<li>(bb) During 1990, subject to the availability of funds.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2)

<ol>

<li>(a) 200</li>

<li>(b) 1 June 1989.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(3) No.

<ol>

<li>(a) The site is too small to accommodate sports facilities.</li>

<li>(b) Owing to other higher priorities, sports facilities are not planned for Iketlo at this stage.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(4) Yes. The local authority has made available a piece of land next to Iketlo.

<ol>

<li>(a) Approximately one hectare</li>

<li>(b) To provide play grounds for pupils.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>OFS: qualifications of teachers</heading>

<question by="#burrows" to="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid">

<num>604.</num>

<from>Mr R M BURROWS</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Education and Development Aid:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) How many teachers employed by his Department at (a) primary and (b) secondary schools in the Orange Free State did not have (i) a matric certificate, (ii) matric plus one year&#x2019;s post-matric training and (iii) matric plus three years&#x2019; post-matric training in 1988;</li>

<li>(2) whether any such teachers were detained by the Police in 1988; if so, how many?</li>

</ol>

<p>B1110E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_education_and_development_aid" to="#burrows">

<from>The MINISTER OF EDUCATION AND DEVELOPMENT AID:</from>

<ol>

<li>(1)

<ol>

<li>(a) Primary teachers*

<ol>

<li>(i) 3 813</li>

<li>(ii) 4 276</li>

<li>(iii) 5 172</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b) Secondary teachers*

<ol>

<li>(i) 94</li>

<li>(ii) 189</li>

<li>(iii) 723<br/>Information as on 1 March 1988. The information provided regards the Orange Free State Region of the Department.<br/>* In all these cases reference is made to teachers in possession of a school-leaving certificate. It is not known how many of these persons obtained a matriculation exemption endorsement as well.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol></li>

<li>(2) None.</li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<heading>Neighbouring states: SA citizens detained</heading>

<question by="#eglin" to="#minister_of_foreign_affairs">

<num>612.</num>

<from>Mr C W EGLIN</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Foreign Affairs:</p>

<p>Whether he will furnish information on South African citizens currently being detained in neighbouring states; if not; why not; if so, (a) how many South African citizens are involved, (b) what are their names, (c) on what dates were they detained, (d) where are they currently being held, (e) what are the reasons given for their detention and (f) what steps are being taken by the South African Government in this regard?</p>

<p>B1118E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_foreign_affairs" to="#eglin">

<from>The MINISTER OF FOREIGN AFFAIRS:</from>

<p>It will not be in the public interest nor in the interest of the persons concerned to publish this information, but the Department of Foreign Affairs will be pleased to furnish available <span class="col_1417-1418" refersTo="page_0747"/>information to the Honourable Member on a confidential basis. I can, however, assure the Honourable Member that the South African Government goes out of its way to look after the interests of those concerned.</p>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Murder of policemen/informers</heading>

<question by="#hulley" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>616.</num>

<from>Mr R R HULLEY</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<p>How many persons charged with the murder of (a) policemen and (b) suspected informers were (i) found guilty, (ii) sentenced to death and (iii) executed in 1988?</p>

<p>B1122E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#hulley">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<ol>

<li>(a)

<ol>

<li>(i) The information is not readily available.</li>

<li>(ii) 6 persons were sentenced to death in 1988 for the murder of policemen.</li>

<li>(iii) 2 persons.</li>

</ol></li>

<li>(b)

<ol>

<li>(i) The information is not readily available.</li>

<li>(ii) 8 persons were sentenced to death in 1988 for the murder of suspected informers.</li>

<li>(iii) None so far.</li>

</ol></li>

</ol>

</answer>

</writtenStatements>

<writtenStatements>

<heading>Vehicles stolen: prosecution/convictions</heading>

<question by="#schwarz" to="#minister_of_justice">

<num>619.</num>

<from>Mr H H SCHWARZ</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Justice:</p>

<p>Whether any (a) prosecutions were instituted and (b) convictions were obtained in respect of motor vehicles reported stolen to the South African Police during (i) the period 1 July 1987 to 30 June 1988 and (ii) 1988; if so, how many in each category as at the latest specified date for which figures are available?</p>

<p>B1125E</p>

</question>

<answer by="#minister_of_justice" to="#schwarz">

<from>The MINISTER OF JUSTICE:</from>

<p>The information is not readily available in the Department. In an effort to be of assistance to the Homourable Member, the following information for the period 1 July 1987 to 30 June 1988 was obtained from the Central Statistical Service:</p>

<ol>

<li>(a) Persons prosecuted for theft of motor vehicles (including motor cycles)</li>

</ol>

<table>

<tr>

<td><p>Whites</p></td>

<td><p>793</p></td>

</tr>

<heading>Bureau for Information: number of publications</heading>

<question by="#soal" to="#minister_of_information_broadcasting_services_and_the_film_industry">

<num>620.</num>

<from>Mr P G SOAL</from>

<p>asked the Minister of Information, Broadcasting Services and the Film Industry:</p>

<ol>

<li>(1) (a) What total number of publications was produced by the Bureau for Information in 1988, (b) what was the title of each publication, (c) what was the total cost of producing each of these publications and (d) who printed each of them;</li>

<li>(2) whether the printing contract in respect of each of these publications was put out to tender; if not, why not; if so,</li>

<li>(3) whether he will furnish details in regard to these tenders; if not, why not; if so, (a) what companies submitted tenders in respect of each publication and (b) what was the amount of each tender?</li>
